[[dtp-dv-verification-plan]]
== DTP OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`dtp`
|Repository |`tt-oca-harness`
|Project |OCAH
|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb and SV-UVM: one environment, two realizations
|Primary simulator |Verilator (cocotb realization); VCS (SV-UVM realization)
|Design specification |`hw/sys/dtp/doc/index.adoc` (includes the `jtag_intf_unit`, `jtag_ptap`, `jtag_stap`, and cross-trigger IP chapters)
|Register sources |`hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_port/regs/cross_trigger_port.rdl`, `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_matrix/regs/cross_trigger_matrix.rdl` (SystemRDL); JTAG TDR layouts in the `jtag_ptap` IP documentation
|===

This VPLAN defines the scenario-level test cases below. The
implementation uses unified OCAH BFM wrappers for standard protocols and
OCAH-local DTP BFMs for DTP-specific protocols.

=== Verification Strategy

The DTP environment is one UVM environment realized in PyUVM on cocotb (Verilator) and in SV-UVM (commercial simulators). Each
test builds `dtp_uvm_top`, brings up `clk_i` and the `pwr_on_rst_ni` / `rst_n_i` resets,
resets the primary TAP, and drives stimulus through the OCAH BFMs:

[arabic]
. *Stimulus*: standard protocols use the unified OCAH VIP wrappers — `ocah_jtag_vip`
(`OcahJtagMasterDriver`) for the JTAG TAP, and `ocah_axi_vip` (`OcahAxiSlaveAgent`, AXI-Lite wrappers,
`OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`) for the SMC fabric / OTP debug and XTRIG CSR buses. DTP-specific
protocols (boundary-scan / STAP loopbacks, IEEE 1687 iJTAG SIB networks, OCAH CTP/CTM)
use OCAH-local models in `env/` (see BFM Policy).
. *Checking*: a scoreboard / reference model compares observed TAP scan data, AXI
transactions, and cross-trigger routing against expected behavior. Unless a procedure
states otherwise, each test ends by requiring zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.
Every expected value has a source other than the RTL: the instruction encodings and
the TAP-state encoding are transcribed from the JTAG interface-unit and PTAP documents,
the cross-trigger register map from the generated SystemRDL collateral and the
network document, and the JTAG2AXI geometries from the `*_JTAG2AXI_CAPS` values the
bridges publish, compared with the DUT every pass. Scan-length evidence
(`CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-COUNT`, `CHK-NONVAC`) counts the TCK
cycles the DUT's exported TAP state spends in Shift-IR or Shift-DR against the width
the sequence drove; a scan-control window records the DUT's shift cycles inside it
(`CHK-SCAN-WIN`), and a chain it judges selected has its select high at every
sample in which the DUT's exported TAP state is Capture-DR through Update-DR
(`CHK-BSR-SELECT`, `CHK-DFT-SIB-SELECT`); a bridge scenario's `CHK-AXI-NONVAC` requires the responder behind
that bridge to have completed a burst in the pass; and every system or power-on
reset a scenario drives records the tb_top reset-assertion counter advancing across
the pulse (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`). On VCS the inline assertions of the vendored
`common_cells` on DTP's AXI, CDC, and cross-trigger paths are compiled in and live
in every test (`COMMON_CELLS_ASSERTS_OFF` in the Integrator Guide's Project
Defines chapter, `doc/integrator/src/defines.adoc`).
. *Debug disables*: DTP takes eleven pre-resolved active-high disables
(`sep_lifecycle_ctrl_pkg::dbg_disable_t`, `dbg_disable_i`); deriving them from
lifecycle policy is SEP-level verification. The testbench bring-up clears all
disables (full debug access) by default; gating scenarios assert exactly the
target's disable field and settle four toggling TCK cycles (the TB margin over
the DUT's 2-stage TCK-domain synchronizers).
. *Coverage*: the covergroups of the `cov/sv/` modules are the functional coverage model,
collected on VCS; Verilator does not compile SystemVerilog covergroups and collects the modules' `cover property` points instead (see `DTP_FCOV.adoc`).
. *Scenario expansion*: multi-scenario families are expanded into separate, named test
cases (see Naming Rules) so each scenario is independently selectable in the
testlists.

=== Naming Rules

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Single-scenario module |Use the module name, for example `dtp_jtag_idcode_test`
|Multi-scenario module |Use `+dtp_<feature>_<scenario>_test+`, for example `dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_test`
|Scenario names |Use descriptive lower-snake-case names that encode operation, mode, direction, or coverage target
|Scenario prefix |Every scenario name starts with `dtp_`, for example `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_write_test`
|Files |Add one test class in `tests/` and one sequence in `seq_lib/` per scenario-expanded test case, in each realization
|===

=== BFM Policy

The cocotb class is named first and its SV-UVM twin second; both realizations bind
the same VIP interfaces `tb_top.sv` instantiates.

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Interface / Protocol |VIP / Model
|Primary JTAG TAP |`ocah_jtag_vip` `OcahJtagMasterDriver` / `ocah_jtag_master_agent`
|AXI4 SMC fabric debug |`ocah_axi_vip` `OcahAxiSlaveAgent` / `ocah_axi_slave_agent`
|AXI4-Lite SMC/SEP OTP debug |`ocah_axi_vip` AXI-Lite wrappers / `ocah_axi_slave_agent` (AXI4-Lite)
|AXI4-Lite XTRIG CSR |`ocah_axi_vip` `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` / `ocah_axi_master_agent` (AXI4-Lite)
|Boundary scan loopback |OCAH-local scan responder in `cocotb/env/` and `uvm/env/`
|STAP host ports |`ocah_jtag_vip` reactive slave devices (one downstream TAP per port, test-attached; wire loopback otherwise)
|IEEE 1687 iJTAG SIB networks |OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model in `cocotb/env/` and `uvm/env/`
|OCAH CTP protocol |OCAH-local CTP BFM in `cocotb/env/` and `uvm/env/`
|OCAH CTM routing |OCAH-local CTM BFM and monitors in `cocotb/env/` and `uvm/env/`
|===

[[dtp-vplan-scope]]
=== Verification Scope

This plan claims DTP behaviour at the DTP boundary, with the DUT elaborated from the
bench configuration table, `tb/dtp_dv_cfg_pkg.sv` mirrored by
`cocotb/env/dtp_dv_cfg.py`: sixteen CTPs, ten internal cross triggers in pulse mode
with their acknowledge unused, nine clock-stop requests, one extra STAP, one IC_RESET
slice per port, every instruction enabled. The JTAG_CAPS and IDCODE scenarios compare
what the DUT publishes with that table. The behaviours below exist only where DTP is
integrated and are the integration level's plan, not this one; a green DTP regression
says nothing about them.

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Behaviour |Why it is out of scope here
|DTP parameterizations other than the bench configuration (`XTRIG_INT_CT_MODE` lanes in handshake mode, the `JTAG_*_ENABLE` flags, the bridge types and pipeline depths, the IDCODE fields) |The bench elaborates one configuration; every entry here is closed for the bench configuration (`tb/dtp_dv_cfg_pkg.sv`) only
|The `dbg_disable_i` producer (deriving the eleven disables from lifecycle policy) |SEP-level verification; the bench drives the pre-resolved vector directly
|The CLA clock-stop loop, the acknowledge handshake of a handshake-mode internal lane, and the CTM lane partition of an integration |Tie-offs and wiring outside DTP
|Address decode on the three JTAG2AXI bridge paths |The bench responders map every address; the fabric's decoder and default subordinate are SMC-level, so the decode scenarios prove responder-injected error handling (section 4)
|A bridge subordinate reset without `rst_n_i` while a bridge transaction is in flight |The bench resets the three bridge responders with `rst_n_i`, the bridges' AXI-side reset, so a reset drops a transaction on both sides of the port; how the subordinates reset relative to `rst_n_i` is set where DTP is integrated
|iJTAG instruments behind the SIBs, real downstream TAPs behind every STAP, and the external STAP chain behind the extended STAP host scan interface |The bench places 4-, 5-, and 6-bit stubs behind the SIBs, attaches behavioural downstream TAPs only in the STAP-selection scenarios and the zero-length-bypass chain leg (0.11), and places a 7-bit host segment behind the extended STAP host scan interface only in the extended-STAP scenario, which every other scenario loops back (section 5)
|The DFT ports `test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni` |Driven inactive from `dtp_tb_if` (functional mode); DFT-mode behaviour is a scan-insertion level concern
|===

[[dtp-scope-traceability]]
=== Scope and Traceability

This section is the requirement-to-test-to-coverage matrix for the debug
and test ports (DTP). The requirements are the designer-owned design
documentation under `hw/sys/dtp/doc/` and the JTAG and cross-trigger IP
chapters it includes; the Test Procedures below state what each canonical
scenario verifies; `DTP_FCOV.adoc` states the coverage intent; live group
membership is `testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes. This
section is the reconciliation between them: which scenarios count as feature
credit, and which requirements have no credit on this bench.

[[dtp-source-of-truth]]
==== Source of truth

The DV book carries no specification or register reference of its own.
Where the documents overlap, the table names the one that wins.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Question |Source of truth |Disposition of the other documents
|What DTP must do |The design documentation under `hw/sys/dtp/doc/` and its IP chapters |This plan cites it and adds no requirement; a plan statement that contradicts a design chapter is a plan defect
|Register and TDR layouts |The `jtag_ptap` architecture chapter (TDRs); the SystemRDL under `hw/ip/cross_trigger/*/regs/` (CSRs) |The DV reference models (`dtp_jtag_ir_model`, `dtp_jtag2axi_model`, `dtp_xtrig_csr_model`) encode these layouts and are checked against them, never the reverse
|Which scenarios exist |`testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists (`jtag.toml`, `debug_tdr.toml`, `jtag2axi.toml`, `scan.toml`, `xtrig.toml`) |The Test Procedures document every enrolled name; a name in one and not the other is a defect in whichever lacks it
|Which scenarios count |The `all` group, `expected_count`-pinned |Every `[[tests]]` entry belongs to at least one scheduled group, so no declared scenario runs only by name (see <<dtp-credit-rules>>)
|Coverage intent per requirement |`DTP_FCOV.adoc` and the `cov/sv/` modules |This matrix names the covergroup bins a row leans on; the bin definitions stay in `DTP_FCOV.adoc`
|Points that cannot fire |The `ignore_bins` of the covergroups, and the hole entries in `cov/config/verilator/coverage_policy.toml` |Each ignore bin states the constraint that excludes it, and each hole entry its structural reason and tracker link; this matrix repeats neither
|===

Requirement identifiers in the matrix are the five feature areas F1–F5.
They partition the design documentation; the only design statements outside
them are the adopter-defined layout of the IC_RESET external slice and the
lifecycle derivation of `dbg_disable_t`, which are integration-level and out
of this bench.

[cols="1,3,2",options="header",]
|===
|Area |Requirement source |Plan sections and credit group
|F1 IEEE 1149.1 TAP and instruction decode |`jtag_ptap`: TAP controller state machine, reset hierarchy, TDR multiplexer, ID code, boundary-scan support, BIST support, test mode persistence (TMP); DTP JTAG integration: reset architecture |Test Procedures section 0; `basic_jtag`
|F2 Debug-control TDRs |`jtag_ptap`: debug control, JTAG capabilities, IC_RESET support, `*_JTAG2AXI_CAPS`; DTP JTAG integration: IC_RESET overrides; DTP clock stop |Test Procedures section 1; `debug_tdr`
|F3 STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scan routing |`jtag_intf_unit`: scan-chain topology, iJTAG network, debug-disable synchronization; `jtag_stap`: 3DCR register, STAP types, debug disable, lockup latches; `jtag_ptap`: 3DIC and iJTAG support; DTP JTAG integration: STAP secondary scan path, debug disable |Test Procedures section 5; `scan`
|F4 JTAG2AXI debug bridges |`jtag_ptap`: JTAG2AXI support (`*_AXI_SINGLE_OP`, `*_AXI_SERIES_*`); `jtag_intf_unit`: JTAG2AXI interfaces; DTP JTAG integration: debug disable, bridge paragraph |Test Procedures sections 2–4; `jtag2axi`
|F5 Cross-trigger network |`cross_trigger_network`: CTP wire-OR and point-to-point modes, CTM, address map, clock-stop control, port indexing; the `cross_trigger_port` and `cross_trigger_matrix` chapters; the SystemRDL |Test Procedures section 6; `xtrig`
|===

[[dtp-live-groups]]
==== Live groups versus credit

Executable membership is resolved from the testlists; each group's
`expected_count` guard pins its size.

[cols="2,4",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Role
|`smoke` |PR gate (`sim.yml`): one basic scenario per feature area, TAP FSM closure and IDCODE (F1), TMP_STATUS (F2), the iJTAG network with every SIB open (F3), an SMC fabric single write and read-back (F4), and CTP wire-OR (F5)
|`functional` |`dtp_sanity_test` and `dtp_jtag_idcode_test` plus the SMC fabric single write and single write-read; bring-up, not a credit set
|`basic_jtag` |F1 credit set: Test Procedures section 0
|`debug_tdr` |F2 credit set: Test Procedures section 1
|`jtag2axi` |F4 credit set: Test Procedures sections 2–4
|`scan` |F3 credit set: Test Procedures section 5
|`xtrig` |F5 credit set: Test Procedures section 6
|`dbg_disable` |Cross-cutting lifecycle-gating closure: the two matrix tests plus every directed gating scenario; every member also belongs to `jtag2axi` or `scan`, so the group adds closure, not names
|`all` |Every canonical scenario, the union of the five credit sets; the `regress.yml` denominator
|===

[[dtp-credit-rules]]
==== Credit rules

* Feature credit requires a canonical scenario in `all`, the named evidence
  it declares (`CHK-*` records finalized once per pass, or a required
  `dtp_scoreboard` feature ending as `CHK-SB-<FEATURE>`), and a
  non-vacuous count; the runner accepts a PASS only with positive
  `results.xml` (cocotb) or `UVM TEST PASSED` (SV-UVM) evidence.
* The cocotb and SV-UVM realizations of one name are one scenario. A row is
  credited once; a realization whose evidence differs is named in the
  row's Checker-evidence cell.
* Coverage credit is the covergroup bins of the `cov/sv/` modules,
  collected on VCS from both realizations and merged. The `cover property`
  points the modules record under Verilator carry no crosses, so they are
  not the same bins and do not stand in for them. A combination no stimulus
  reaches on the bench configuration is an `ignore_bins` entry of its
  covergroup, and no credited row leans on a waiver.
* The 22-cell debug-disable contract is credited only through the two
  matrix tests: in cocotb their checker-gated ledger refuses to finish with a
  missing cell, and in SV-UVM every pass runs each field's one-hot row and both
  boundary rows; the directed gating scenarios corroborate individual cells.
* The formal property modules under `formal/props/` corroborate the rows the
  plan's target table names, the TAP controller module the F1 state-machine
  rows; `run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal` runs them as the `*_fpv` items of
  `testlists/formal.toml` (<<dtp-formal-plan>>), and they are not regression
  credit.
* Deriving `dbg_disable_t` from lifecycle policy, the adopter-defined layout
  of the IC_RESET external slice, and real SMC and SEP debug TAPs behind the
  STAPs are integration-level: OUT. The downstream TAPs the scan scenarios and the
  zero-length-bypass scenario attach are behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` devices,
  and the iJTAG chains loop back in `tb_top`.

[[dtp-traceability-matrix]]
==== Matrix (F1–F5)

Every canonical scenario in `all` appears in one row; the CLA and random
clock-stop scenarios also carry F5.7, the
`+dtp_jtag2axi_<target>_series_write{,_read}_incr_with_error_test+` scenarios
also carry F4.3, and `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test` also
carries F4.4. Test names written with `<target>`
expand over `smc_axi`, `smc_otp_axi`, and `sep_otp_axi`.

[cols="2,3,1,2,3,1",options="header",]

|F1.3 IDCODE: default after reset, explicit load, field values, marker bit |`dtp_jtag_idcode_test` |smoke, functional, basic_jtag |`cg_tap_smoke` `cp_idcode_read` and `cp_idcode_marker`; `cg_dr_scan` `cp_dr_instruction` `idcode` |`CHK-IDCODE-MARKER`, `CHK-IDCODE-MANUFACTURER`, `CHK-IDCODE-PART-NUMBER`, `CHK-IDCODE-VERSION`, `CHK-IDCODE-RAW`, `CHK-IDCODE-STABLE`, `CHK-IDCODE-RECOVERY`, `CHK-RESET-COUNT`; `idcode` scoreboard feature |YES

|F4.3 Response status: SLVERR and DECERR on single and series operations, the error-status TDR, recovery after an error |`+dtp_jtag2axi_<target>_error_{single_write,single_read,series_incr_write,series_incr_read,series_no_incr_write,series_no_incr_read,series_incr_write_with_status,series_incr_read_with_status}_test+`, `+dtp_jtag2axi_<target>_series_write{,_read}_incr_with_error_test+`, `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test` |jtag2axi |`cg_jtag2axi_response` `x_target_outcome` `slverr` and `decerr`; `cg_jtag2axi_recovery` `x_target_prior_path`; `cg_jtag2axi_series` `x_mode_beat` `fault` |Armed non-OKAY response (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM), `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-STRB` (scenarios with a single-operation write), `CHK-AXI-RDATA` (scenarios with reads), `CHK-AXI-WMEM` (recovery writes and the with-error series write beats, against the stimulus), `CHK-J2A-BUS-REQ` (every scenario but the decode-error ones, fault beats included), `CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS` (the sequence's judgement of the SINGLE_OP or SERIES_CTRL status: the injected code right after the fault beat, OKAY after a plain series beat before it), `CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT` (the per-shift status bit of the with-error series and of the with-status error series), `CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA` (the seeded word of the errored read beat on the single-read, plain series read, and decode-error read scenarios), `CHK-J2A-SERIES-ADDR` (the SERIES_CTRL address after the plain series error beats and after the fault beat of the with-status write error); `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` and `CHK-AXI-CREDITS` (cocotb); `CHK-AXI-WADDR` and `CHK-AXI-WDATA` on the single-operation writes, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` on the reads, and the `jtag2axi_req` and `jtag2axi_status` scoreboard features (SV-UVM) |YES

`CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA` on the entry sweep's errored reads; on the back-to-back leaf's armed SLVERR, the TAP-reset legs' armed SLVERR and DECERR, and the entry sweep's armed codes, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` (cocotb) and `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` (SV-UVM); ==== XTRIG, CTP, and CTM Scenarios

CTM port naming follows the cross-trigger matrix register map
(`hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_matrix/regs/cross_trigger_matrix.rdl`): a
CT_Src port is a matrix output, the port that delivers a trigger, and a CT_Dst
port is a matrix input, the port a trigger enters on.
`+CT_SRC[k]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` therefore holds one bit per input port
whose pulses are OR'd onto output `k`. The CTM procedures below name the
register by the output that must fire (`dst`) and its bits by the inputs that
fire it (`src`); a multicast is one register per selected output, each holding
the same source-input bit.

[cols=",,",options="header",]
