The bridge's TCK side resets with the TAP's Test-Logic-Reset and its CDC runs the isolate-and-clear, while the bridge's AXI side and the responders keep running, where the system reset of the legs above resets both.
* Four drain points per bridge and direction, in a seeded order. Inside the clear: the stall released six to sixteen system cycles after the last TCK cycle of the reset, while the TAP still holds Test-Logic-Reset, so the response returns while the AXI side's clear is pending. After the clear: released once the clear has finished, 64 to 96 TCK cycles in Run-Test/Idle after Test-Logic-Reset. Queued: as after the clear, with a SINGLE_OP of the new session in the same direction issued first (a write, or a read of a slot holding a seeded word, at a seeded beat of a second window with seeded address bits above the responder window), so the new request waits in the CDC's destination spill register until the held one is accepted. Aligned: the stall released from two system cycles before to three after the TRST assertion; each pass starts at its scenario seed modulo six and each bridge and direction takes the next offset, so the sixteen passes walk every offset on every leg and a response can coincide with the cycle the AXI side's clear starts.
* Each leg draws a seeded SLVERR or DECERR. The responder answers the held read with it and the held write with OKAY, a queued read with OKAY and a queued write with the leg's error, and the follow-on operation with the other error code, so no two completions of a direction in a leg share a response.
* An AXI manager keeps a request valid until it is accepted, so the held request is never withdrawn: it completes on the bus exactly once after the release, a write lands its word, and the bridge consumes the response on its AXI side. The SINGLE_OP status after the drain reads the `SUCCESS` the TAP reset leaves, with no operation pending, where a held read's response reaching the JTAG side would read its injected error. The `jtag2axi_req` scoreboard feature pairs the held request's prediction with its late completion, and the `jtag2axi_status` feature predicts every capture with that completion consumed on the AXI side.
* The next operation in the same direction is answered with the other error code, and a read with a seeded errored-beat word; it reports that status and word, so no earlier response of its direction satisfied it, and it is the only further completion of its direction on the port.
* The queued request completes after the held one with its own status and data: it is the newest completion of its direction, a write reports the leg's error, and a read reports `SUCCESS` with its slot's word.
* After the TAP-reset legs, a recovery write and read on every bridge complete with OKAY and the expected data.
* A bridge that withdraws a held request, or lands it twice, fails `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN`; one that passes the held request's response to the JTAG side fails `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DISCARD`, or `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` and the `jtag2axi_status` comparison when the queued request takes it; one that issues the queued request ahead of the held one fails `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` and the `jtag2axi_req` pairing. A run with `DTP_J2A_ORPHAN_NEGATIVE` set fails every `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-*` record.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each bridge: hold the responder's AW channel with a READY stall that outlasts the scenario, record the target slot's prior word, and issue a SINGLE_OP write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`. The scenario aborts this write, so it arms no stimulus-intent record; `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` judges its slot, before and after the follow-on write.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle while sampling the bridge FSM, the pending flag, and the port's AW stall counter through `dtp_tb_if` until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE` and the stall counter advances, bounded in TCK cycles (the FSM and the CDC's TCK side advance only while TCK runs); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for a single system cycle (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`), then release the stall.
. Step TCK while sampling the FSM until it returns to `AXI_IDLE`, for at most 128 TCK cycles; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`. Record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky clear-seen flag and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` from the unchanged target slot.
. Step 128 more TCK cycles so the first clear completes, which the PTAP document bounds at 52 TCK cycles on this bench, where TCK is the slower clock. Clear the clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for a single system cycle again (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`), step 32 TCK cycles, and record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` for the second clear.
. Capture the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times, stopping when it leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL`; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `DECERR`, the status of an operation a system reset discarded, and when it matches, issue a legal write to a clean slot with the memory compare and record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` on the aborted slot again.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges that did not report the discarded write.
. For each bridge: preload a slot, arm the read reset for the bridge, and issue a SINGLE_OP read of the slot; step TCK until the tb_top reset-assertion counter advances (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`), then disarm.
a. Step TCK until the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE` (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`) and record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`; capture the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times and record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `DECERR` and against a zero data field, then read the slot back with OKAY and its word.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges that did not report the discarded read.
. For the address-phase legs, for each bridge, a write then a read: record the slot's prior word (a read's slot preloaded with a seeded word), load the bridge's SINGLE_OP instruction, and shift the operation in as raw TMS steps that stop with the TAP in Update-DR. The operation arms no stimulus-intent record, since it never reaches the bus.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag and pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three system cycles with TCK idle (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`).
. Step TCK twice: the bridge latches the operation on the first edge and its state machine enters the address state on the second. Record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` for the state machine on the operation's path with the pending flag set and no request counted at the port since the pulse, which clears the port's request counters.
. Step TCK once more and record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` for the state machine back in `AXI_IDLE` with nothing pending, and `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky flag.
. Step 32 TCK cycles so the TCK-side clear completes; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` for no request counted at the port since the pulse and the slot's word unchanged.
. Capture the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times, stopping when it leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL`; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `DECERR`, and for a read against a zero data field, and when it matches, issue a recovery access of the same direction to a clean slot with the memory or data compare (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`).
. After all six legs, fail the pass naming the legs that did not report the discarded operation.
. For the TAP-reset legs, for each bridge and direction, at each drain point in the seeded order: hold the responder's AR channel (read) or AW and W channels (write) with a READY stall that outlasts the leg, arm the leg's error with a seeded errored-beat word for a read, and issue a SINGLE_OP through `OcahJtagMasterDriver` with its stimulus-intent records armed, since it completes on the bus.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle until the bridge FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE` and the held channel's stall counter advances; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag.
. Reset the TAP: assert TRST, clock one to four TCK cycles under it and keep it asserted, or step five TMS-high cycles. At the aligned drain point, assert TRST and release the stall at the leg's offset, both on system clock edges.
a. Inside the clear: wait six to sixteen system cycles after the last TCK cycle of the reset, release the stall, wait for the port to complete the held request, and record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN` against the TAP still in Test-Logic-Reset.
. Release TRST, leave Test-Logic-Reset, step 64 to 96 TCK cycles in Run-Test/Idle, and record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`.
a. After the clear and queued: clear the clear-seen flag and record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN` when it stays clear over four system cycles while the held channel's stall counter keeps advancing.
b. Queued: issue a SINGLE_OP of the same direction to a seeded address in a second window, a read of a slot preloaded with a seeded word or a write armed with the leg's error; step TCK until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE` and two cycles more, wait sixteen system cycles, and record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` for the FSM waiting with the operation pending while the held request still stalls.
c. After the clear and queued: release the stall.
. Wait for the port to complete the held request, and the queued one, step TCK until the pending flag falls, and capture SINGLE_OP up to eight times. Record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN` for one completion of the direction, two with a queued request, and for a write the held word in its slot (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`); record the newest completion's address under `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN`, or under `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` as the queued request's. Record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DISCARD` against `SUCCESS`, or `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` against the queued request's status, the leg's error for a write and `SUCCESS` with its slot's word in the data field for a read.
. Arm the other error code, and for a read a seeded errored-beat word, on a third slot; issue one operation of the same direction there and capture SINGLE_OP. Each armed response is consumed by the completion it was armed for (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM). Record `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DISCARD` for the injected status and errored word, and `CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN` for exactly one more completion of the direction.
. After the TAP-reset legs, issue a recovery write and read on every bridge with the memory and data compares (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`).
. After the pass, fail it naming the legs with a failed judgement.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` |The FSM has left `AXI_IDLE`, the pending flag is set, and the held AW channel's stall counter has advanced when the reset lands; on the read-response leg, the reset asserted on the AR handshake; on the address-phase legs, the FSM in the operation's address state with the pending flag set and no request on the port; on the TAP-reset legs, the held channel's stall counter has advanced when the TAP reset lands
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` |The FSM is back in `AXI_IDLE` within 128 TCK cycles after the reset; on the address-phase legs, one TCK edge after the address state, with nothing pending
|`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` |The bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after each system reset and after the TAP reset
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` |The aborted operation's slot in the subordinate memory is unchanged, after the reset and after the follow-on write; on the address-phase legs, no AW, W or AR reaches the port
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` |After the second reset, the SINGLE_OP status leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight SINGLE_OP captures and reads `DECERR` for the write the first reset discarded; the follow-on legal write then completes with OKAY and lands (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`); on the read-response leg, SINGLE_OP reads `DECERR` with a zero data field for the discarded read; on the address-phase legs, SINGLE_OP reads `DECERR` for the operation discarded in its address state, with a zero data field for a read, and the recovery access completes
|`CHK-RESET-COUNT` |The tb_top reset-assertion counter advanced by one across each pulse
|`CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DRAIN` |The held request completes on the port exactly once after the release, at its address, and a write leaves its word in the slot; the release falls where the drain point puts it (the TAP still in Test-Logic-Reset inside the clear; the clear finished with the request still held after it); the follow-on operation is the only further completion of the direction
|`CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-DISCARD` |The SINGLE_OP status after the drain reads `SUCCESS` with no operation pending, not the held read's injected error; the follow-on operation reports its own injected code and, for a read, the errored-beat word
|`CHK-J2A-ORPHAN-ORDER` |The queued request waits behind the held one with the FSM busy, completes after it as the newest completion of its direction, and reports its own response: the leg's error for a write, `SUCCESS` and its slot's word for a read
|`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` (cocotb), `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` (SV-UVM) |Each armed SLVERR or DECERR, on the held read, the queued write and the follow-on operation, is consumed by the response it was armed for
|`CHK-AXI-WMEM` |The held write's slot holds the word it wrote; the system-reset leg's follow-on legal write and the recovery writes land
|`CHK-AXI-RDATA` |The recovery reads return their slot's word
|===

*Pass Criteria*: On every bridge the first reset lands with the FSM observed mid-flight, the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE`, both CDC clears are observed, no write escapes, and the status still reads `DECERR` for the discarded write after the second reset so the follow-on write completes; on the address-phase legs, the clear returns every bridge's FSM from its address state with no request on the port and SINGLE_OP reads `DECERR`; on the TAP-reset legs, in both directions and at every drain point, the held request completes on the fabric exactly once, its response never reaches the JTAG side, a queued request completes after it with its own status and data, and the next operation reports its own response, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-432-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test]]
===== 4.32 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Back-to-back CDC reset recovery, and resets inside a running clear sequence

*Test Coverage*:

* Two system-reset pulses back to back across the TCK-to-ACLK domains on an idle bridge, the second inside the first's settle time
* A system-reset pulse placed inside the ACLK-side clear sequence that a single-cycle pulse with TCK idle restarted, in the `CLEAR`, `WAIT_CLEAR_PHASE_ACK`, `POST_CLEAR` or `FINISHED` phase of the reset controller, a choice per bridge and pass that rotates with the scenario seed so the passes visit every phase on every bridge. The three bridges' CDC controllers share the system reset, the system clock, TCK and the TAP's Test-Logic-Reset, so the phase of one controller, exported by `tb_top` through `dtp_tb_if`, is the phase of every bridge's controllers; the sequence steps TCK in Run-Test/Idle until that phase is observed and asserts the reset on the following system clock edge. The TCK side stays isolated across the restart, the status completed before the burst survives it, and the recovery accesses prove both sides released their isolation.
* A TAP reset asserted in the one TCK cycle the TCK-side four-phase receiver spends waiting for its isolate acknowledge after a system reset (the third TCK cycle after the pulse, with TCK idle before it), and a system reset asserted in the one system clock cycle the ACLK-side receiver spends there after a TAP reset (the third system clock cycle after TRST falls); both on idle bridges, each followed by the clear completing, a SINGLE_OP capture reading the `SUCCESS` the reset leaves with no operation pending, and a recovery write and read on every bridge
* A SINGLE_OP write completed with an injected SLVERR before the burst: the resets discard nothing, so the SINGLE_OP status still reads `SLVERR` after them
* The TCK-side isolate-and-clear of every bridge's CDC controller runs after the burst and after each reset placed in a receiver's waiting cycle (the sticky `cdc_clear_seen` observable), and every pulse is counted (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`)
* After the burst and after each receiver-wait reset the bridge's state machine is idle with no operation pending (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`); the FIFOs' settled state is judged by the post-recovery reads and writes completing with the expected status and data
* Post-recovery reads and writes complete correctly across all involved clock domains; a reset that lands on a bridge with a write in flight is the mid-flight abort scenarios' claim, not this one

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm a one-shot SLVERR on a slot of the bridge's target and issue a SINGLE_OP write to it; the write completes with `SLVERR`, consuming the armed response.
. With the bridge idle, clear the sticky `cdc_clear_seen` flags and pulse `rst_n_i` for one system cycle with TCK idle (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`): every ACLK-side reset controller restarts in `ISOLATE`, its request held until TCK runs.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle, at most 64 cycles, until `dtp_tb_if` shows the ACLK-side controller in the pass's phase; on the following falling system clock edge sample the phase observable and pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three cycles (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`); record `CHK-J2A-CDC-PHASE` from the sample. Step 64 more TCK cycles so the restarted sequence completes.
. Clear the sticky `cdc_clear_seen` flags.
. Apply two system-reset pulses back to back through `dtp_tb_if`, the second within the first's settle time, each counted by the tb_top reset-assertion counter.
. Step TCK and confirm every bridge's CDC controller ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear (`cdc_clear_seen`); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` for the state machine idle with no operation pending.
. Capture the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times, stopping when it leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL`; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SLVERR`.
. Issue subsequent write and read operations and confirm each reaches its expected terminal status with correct data.
. After all three bridges, clear the sticky flags, pulse `rst_n_i` for one system cycle with TCK idle, step exactly three TCK cycles, sample the TCK-side receiver observable and assert TRST, clocking one to four TCK cycles under it; release TRST, leave Test-Logic-Reset and step 64 TCK cycles. On every bridge record `CHK-J2A-CDC-PHASE` from the sample, `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`, capture SINGLE_OP up to eight times and record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SUCCESS`, then issue a recovery write and read (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`).
. Clear the sticky flags; half a system cycle after a system clock edge assert TRST, clocking one to four TCK cycles under it; three rising system clock edges after TRST falls, on the following falling edge, sample the ACLK-side receiver observable and pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three cycles (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`); release TRST, leave Test-Logic-Reset and step 64 TCK cycles; then the records and recovery accesses of the previous step.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_clear_chk` |Every bridge's CDC controller ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset burst and after each receiver-wait reset (`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`), and every pulse was counted (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`)
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_phase_chk` |The pulse placed inside the clear sequence lands in the phase the pass selected, and the four-phase receivers' waiting cycle takes the reset of its own side (`CHK-J2A-CDC-PHASE`); every bridge's state machine is idle with no operation pending after each reset (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`)
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_status_chk` |The SINGLE_OP status still reads the `SLVERR` completed before the burst, and reads `SUCCESS` after each receiver-wait reset (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`)
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_recovery_chk` |Post-recovery reads/writes complete correctly with expected status and data after the burst and after each receiver-wait reset (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Back-to-back resets on an idle bridge, one of them inside the running ACLK-side clear sequence in the pass's phase, run every CDC controller's isolate-and-clear and are each counted, the SLVERR completed before them survives them, the resets placed in the receivers' waiting cycles leave every bridge idle with its status at `SUCCESS`, and subsequent reads and writes complete with the expected status and data on every bridge, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-433-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test]]
===== 4.33 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Bridge write DECERR handling, response injected by the responder, all three targets

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op write on each of the three bridges (SMC fabric AXI4, SMC OTP and SEP OTP AXI4-Lite) answered with DECERR by that target's `ocah_axi_vip` responder; the bridge ports leave the DTP straight into the responders, so the DTP boundary has no address decoder and no unmapped address, and an interconnect's decode-miss and default-slave behaviour is SoC-level verification
* The write status captures DECERR in op[1:0] and the scoreboard classifies the observed DECERR as the armed response
* The errored slot keeps its prior word (the responder drops an armed write beat)
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal write on every target

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. On each target's responder, arm a one-shot DECERR write response at a beat-aligned slot and record the slot's word.
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op write to that slot on the bridge; poll the op-status until not Busy and confirm it reports DECERR.
. Confirm the slot's word is unchanged.
. Issue a follow-on legal write on the same bridge and confirm OKAY with the intent bytes landing in the responder memory.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_decerr_wr_status_chk` |The write status reports DECERR for the responder-injected response on every target, and the observed DECERR is classified as armed (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM)
|`j2a_decerr_wr_mem_chk` |The errored slot's word is unchanged after the DECERR write
|`j2a_decerr_wr_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY and lands its bytes (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every bridge reports the injected DECERR, no memory side effect lands at the errored slot, and a follow-on legal write succeeds on every target, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-434-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test]]
===== 4.34 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Bridge read DECERR handling, response injected by the responder, all three targets

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op read on each of the three bridges (SMC fabric AXI4, SMC OTP and SEP OTP AXI4-Lite) answered with DECERR by that target's `ocah_axi_vip` responder; the bridge ports leave the DTP straight into the responders, so the DTP boundary has no address decoder and no unmapped address, and an interconnect's decode-miss and default-slave behaviour is SoC-level verification
* The read status captures DECERR in op[1:0], and the read-data field returns the RDATA of the errored beat (the bridge latches the beat's data with its status): the word the responder drove on that beat, not the preloaded slot value
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal read on every target

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. On each target's responder, preload a nonzero word in a beat-aligned slot and arm a one-shot DECERR read response there; the errored beat carries a seeded nonzero word that differs from the preload.
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op read of that slot on the bridge; poll the op-status until not Busy and confirm it reports DECERR.
. Confirm the read-data field equals the seeded word, which the passive monitor observed on the errored beat, and differs from the preloaded word.
. Issue a follow-on legal read of a preloaded slot on the same bridge and confirm OKAY with matching data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_decerr_rd_status_chk` |The read status reports DECERR for the responder-injected response on every target, and the observed DECERR is classified as armed (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM)
|`j2a_decerr_rd_rdata_chk` |`CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA`: returned read data equals the seeded word the errored beat carried and differs from the preloaded slot value
|`j2a_decerr_rd_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the preloaded data with OKAY (`CHK-AXI-RDATA`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every bridge reports the injected DECERR, the read-data field carries the errored beat's RDATA, and a follow-on legal read succeeds on every target, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-435-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test]]
===== 4.35 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Interleaved clean and DECERR-faulted accesses on every bridge

*Test Coverage*:

* The bridge AXI ports leave the DTP boundary straight into the bench responders, so there is no decoder or default subordinate at this level; each responder is configured to answer DECERR for one slot while the neighbouring slot stays clean
* Clean (OKAY) and faulted (DECERR) accesses alternate back to back on each of the three bridges, reads and writes
* Per-access status is preserved across the interleave
* No cross-contamination: a faulted access neither disturbs the clean slot's data nor corrupts the status of the adjacent clean access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build an interleaved access stream through the JTAG2AXI path:
a. Alternate accesses to a clean slot with accesses to the slot the responder is configured to fault with DECERR, covering both reads and writes.
. Drive the stream and capture per-access outcomes:
a. Confirm each clean access completes with OKAY and the expected data or side effect.
b. Confirm each faulted access reports DECERR in the SINGLE_OP status.
. Verify ordering and isolation:
a. Confirm status is preserved across the interleave and that the clean slot's data is unaffected by the interleaved faulted accesses.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_mixed_status_chk` |Each clean access reports OKAY and each faulted access reports DECERR
|`j2a_mixed_order_chk` |Per-access status preserved across the interleave
|`j2a_mixed_isolation_chk` |Clean slot data unaffected by the interleaved faulted accesses (`CHK-AXI-RDATA`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The interleave exercises the clean path and the responder-injected DECERR path on every bridge with correct per-access status, and the clean slot's data is uncontaminated.

'''''

[[sec-436-dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test]]
===== 4.36 `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Series corner cases and the CDC FIFO entry sweep across all bridges

*Test Coverage*:

* Incrementing and fixed-address write series on each enabled bridge: the SERIES_CTRL address captured after the series and the memory every beat lands
* Held series status: one faulted beat sets the SERIES_CTRL status, the status holds across the clean beats that follow, and `SERIES_CTRL.reset` clears it for the next series
* Per-bridge independence: the three bridges' series beats landed interleaved in seeded order leave every bridge's captured address and every written word exact
* A bridge whose `SERIES_CTRL.op` reads OKAY after the clean beats that follow a faulted beat fails `CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS`: the PTAP document holds the SLVERR or DECERR code until `SERIES_CTRL.reset`
* The bench does not check the series FIFO depth corners or `SERIES_CTRL.pl_depth` read pipelining, and every series starts aligned to its bridge's beat size
* The CDC FIFO entry sweep: every entry of the five CDC FIFOs of each bridge's clock crossing written at least twice in one TAP session. A FIFO pushes into its entries in turn from its last clear, and its entry count N is the power of two at or above the bridge's read pipeline depth plus two: eight on the SMC fabric and SEP OTP bridges, four on the SMC OTP bridge. Each bridge takes 2N + 2 checked SINGLE_OP writes and as many reads
* Seeded payloads on every access: address bits above the responder window and a beat inside it; on the AXI4 fabric port a byte offset inside the beat with a transfer size the offset is aligned to, on the AXI-Lite OTP ports a word-aligned address with a SINGLE_OP size field of one, two or four bytes, the port carrying the four-byte beat whatever the field holds, writes and reads alike; the data, the write strobes and the preloaded read word
* One SLVERR or DECERR per FIFO entry on a seeded visit, for the writes and for the reads, the two codes alternating across entries from a seeded phase, so every entry stores an error code and OKAY in turn; an errored read returns the seeded word the errored beat carried, and the last read on each bridge is an errored one
* A CDC FIFO entry keeps its payload until the asynchronous reset of its FIFO's source side: the TAP reset for the AW, W and AR FIFOs, whose source is the TCK side, and the system reset for the B and R FIFOs. The TAP reset and then the system reset land while every entry holds its last payload, and each runs the CDC's isolate-and-clear with the bridge's state machine idle
* The bench does not sample the CDC FIFO entries; the sweep is judged by every access's status and data, the CDC clear and idle state machine after each reset, the SINGLE_OP status after them, and the recovery write and read
* The SINGLE_OP status, an error code before the TAP reset, reads OKAY after it (the `op` field's reset value), and the system reset on the idle bridge discards nothing and keeps it; a bridge whose TAP reset leaves the error code fails `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`
* A recovery write and read complete on every bridge after the two resets

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. On each of the three enabled JTAG2AXI bridges, through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Reset the series state, program an incrementing write series of a seeded beat count, land every beat with its word compared in the subordinate memory, and capture SERIES_CTRL: the address advanced by the beat count and status OKAY (`CHK-J2A-SERIES-ADDR`, `CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS`).
b. Program a fixed-address write series and land the same beat count: the memory holds the last beat and the captured address is the programmed one.
. On each bridge, arm one SLVERR or DECERR beat of a fresh incrementing series and land every beat: the faulted beat leaves its slot untouched, the capture right after it and the capture after the last beat both carry the injected code with the advanced address (the `op` status holds until `SERIES_CTRL.reset`, PTAP document), and after `SERIES_CTRL.reset` a fresh one-beat series captures OKAY.
. Program an incrementing series on all three bridges, land their beats interleaved in seeded order, and capture each bridge: its own address progression and every one of its words exact.
. Reset the TAP and enter Run-Test/Idle; no TAP reset follows until step 6.
. On each of the three enabled JTAG2AXI bridges, N being its CDC FIFO entry count, draw for the writes and for the reads the visit on which each entry takes its SLVERR or DECERR, the last read being an errored one; then for each push j from 0 to 2N+1, through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Issue a SINGLE_OP write. An OKAY write carries a seeded address, size, data and strobe pattern and lands its enabled bytes in the subordinate memory; an errored write is a full beat answered with the armed code, reports that code and leaves its slot unchanged.
b. Issue a SINGLE_OP read. An OKAY read returns, on the lanes its address and size select, the seeded word preloaded in its beat; an errored read is a full beat answered with the armed code and a seeded word, and its status and data field report both (`CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA`).
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, reset the TAP, step TCK for 128 cycles in Run-Test/Idle, and on every bridge record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`.
. Clear the flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three system cycles (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`), step TCK for 128 cycles, and on every bridge record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`.
. On every bridge capture the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times, stopping when it leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL`, and record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against OKAY; then issue a recovery write with the memory compare and a recovery read of a preloaded word.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_series_addr_chk` |SERIES_CTRL captures the beat-count-advanced address after an incrementing series and the programmed address after a fixed-address series (`CHK-J2A-SERIES-ADDR`)
|`j2a_series_status_chk` |The injected code is captured after the faulted beat and the clean beats that follow it; the capture after `SERIES_CTRL.reset` and a fresh beat reads OKAY (`CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS`)
|`j2a_series_isolation_chk` |Interleaving the three bridges' beats leaves each bridge's captured address and every written word exact
|`j2a_cdc_sweep_access_chk` |Every write and read of the sweep reports its expected status: OKAY writes land their enabled bytes, errored writes leave their slot unchanged (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`), OKAY reads return the preloaded lanes (`CHK-AXI-RDATA`), errored reads return the errored beat's word (`CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA`), and every armed code is classified as expected (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM)
|`j2a_cdc_sweep_clear_chk` |After the TAP reset and after the system reset, every bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear (`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`) and its state machine is idle (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`); the system-reset pulse was counted (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`)
|`j2a_cdc_sweep_status_chk` |The SINGLE_OP status reads OKAY after the two resets, the last read before them having reported an error code (`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`)
|`j2a_cdc_sweep_recovery_chk` |The recovery write lands (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`) and the recovery read returns the preloaded word (`CHK-AXI-RDATA`) on every bridge
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The address progression, the held status and its clear, and the interleaved-series memory hold on each of the three bridges; every access of the sweep reports its expected status and data on every bridge, both resets clear every bridge's CDC with its state machine idle, the SINGLE_OP status reads OKAY after them, and the recovery write and read complete, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-437-dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test]]
===== 4.37 `dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Debug-disable matrix over the three JTAG2AXI bridge gate fields

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic one-hot rows for `smc_jtag2axi`, `smc_otp_jtag2axi`, and `sep_otp_jtag2axi`, plus the all-clear and all-disabled boundary masks and seeded multi-hot masks
* Per row and target: an allowed bridge completes a write+readback with exactly that request activity; a blocked bridge shows zero request activity from a counter snapshot taken before the gated TDR write, puts no transaction inside a scoreboard blocked window held across the disable release, keeps the SINGLE_OP image captured before the disable while gated (`CHK-J2A-GATE-TDR`), and keeps its RAM sentinel intact both while gated and after the release (no delayed replay), then recovers with exactly one sanctioned write and read
* Mixed rows prove unrelated-target isolation (an enabled bridge keeps working while another is gated)
* In cocotb every checker-verified outcome samples its `DtpDbgDisableFcov` ledger cell, and the test requires all six bridge cells and emits the JSON coverage artifact; in both realizations `dtp_jtag2axi_fcov` records the same cells at the signal level in `cg_dbg_disable_jtag2axi`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each row mask (one-hot per field, all-clear, all-disabled, seeded multi-hot): before the row's disables, capture the SINGLE_OP reference image of each bridge the row gates (an all-zero request, then a NOP capture); drive the full disable vector and settle four toggling TCK cycles.
. For each bridge target under the row mask: if enabled, complete a write+readback with exact activity-delta evidence; if gated, preload a RAM sentinel, snapshot the request counters, open a blocked window on the target's stream, shift a full-strobe write request, and verify the counters unchanged, the sentinel intact, and the gated request's capture and a NOP capture after it equal the reference image.
. After each row with gated targets: clear all disables without reset, verify the counters are still unchanged from the pre-attempt snapshot, close each blocked window with zero transactions inside it, verify every sentinel is still intact (no delayed replay), then complete a sanctioned recovery write+readback per previously-gated target; from the pre-attempt snapshot to the end of that recovery the target shows exactly that write and that read, and its sentinel is still intact.
. In cocotb, emit the `DtpDbgDisableFcov` JSON artifact and require all six bridge cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_matrix_allow_chk` |An enabled bridge completes write+readback with exactly that request activity (`CHK-AXI-NOACT` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-GATE-EXACT` SV-UVM)
|`j2a_matrix_block_chk` |A gated bridge's request counters are unchanged from the snapshot taken before its TDR write, the RAM sentinel is intact, and the gated request's capture and a NOP capture after it equal the SINGLE_OP image captured before the disable (`CHK-J2A-GATE-TDR`)
|`j2a_matrix_noreplay_chk` |After the disables release the counters are still unchanged from that snapshot, the blocked window held across the release closes with zero monitored transactions, and the sentinels are intact (no delayed replay); through the recovery the target shows exactly the sanctioned write and read
|`j2a_matrix_recovery_chk` |A sanctioned operation succeeds on every previously-gated bridge
|`j2a_matrix_isolation_chk` |Enabled bridges keep working while others are gated
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every row mask produces the per-field allowed/blocked outcomes with no delayed replay and full recovery, closing all six bridge cells of `DtpDbgDisableFcov` and `cg_dbg_disable_jtag2axi`, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-5-stap-3dcr-and-ijtag-scenarios]]
==== 5. STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG Scenarios

[[sec-51-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test]]
===== 5.1 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs OFF

*Test Coverage*:

* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with every SIB control bit driven to 0 yields the minimum-length scan chain
* DR chain length equals the SIB-control bits only (3 bits for sec_dft, dft, dfd) with none of the bench's instrument stubs (4, 5, 6 bits behind sec_dft, dft, dfd) inserted
* None of `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` assert during Shift-DR
* Closed SIBs bypass their instrument stubs so no stub capture/update occurs, whatever disable mask is in force (the stubs are the bench's 4-, 5-, and 6-bit registers behind the SIBs; see Verification Scope)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) into the PTAP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` and program a seeded non-zero SIB pattern, so the all-off result is a transition from open SIBs.
. Shift a composed SIB pattern with all three SIB bits = 0 and pulse Update-DR; check the captured bits against the SIB model.
. Measure the active DR chain length.
a. Shift a seeded marker word ahead of the chain image and locate its MSB in the TDO stream.
b. Verify the latency equals the 3 SIB-control bits only (no instrument register widths added).
. Under a temporal window over the whole scan, verify no SIB select pulses (every stub stays out of the chain); the window also records the DUT's Shift-DR cycles, so a TAP that never shifted cannot satisfy the quiet checks.
. Repeat the all-off programming under a seeded per-SIB disable mask and verify the same outcome.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_all_off_len_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-LEN`: the marker latency through the SELECT_IJTAG chain equals the 3 SIB bits with every instrument stub bypassed
|`sib_all_off_ctrl_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: no DFD/DFT/secure-DFT `select` pulses during the observe scan, and a gated SIB's `shift_en`/`capture_en`/`update_en` stay quiet
|`sib_all_off_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the captured SIB states (all 0) and the marker passthrough match the SIB reference model on every scan, including the transition from the seeded open set
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Chain length collapses to the SIB-control bits, all instrument scan controls stay inactive, and observed data matches the SIB reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-52-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test]]
===== 5.2 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs ON

*Test Coverage*:

* Before the first scan, with every disable clear, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_out_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_out_o`, `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o`, and `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o` drive a resolved 0 (the SIB and 3DCR scan stages reset)
* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with all SIB bits = 1 produces the full-length iJTAG scan chain
* DR chain length equals sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths (3 SIBs {plus} 3 instruments)
* All three scan controls (`jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o`) assert during Shift-DR
* Asserting all three SIB disables (`dbg_disable_i.dft_secure`, `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure`, `dbg_disable_i.dfd`) forces the SIBs closed even when the scan pattern requests all-ON, collapsing the chain
* Access is restored when the disables clear

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. After Test-Logic-Reset with every disable clear, and before any IR or DR scan, sample the three iJTAG host scan outputs and the extended STAP host scan output and verify each is a resolved 0.
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Shift a pattern enabling all SIBs (all bits = 1); Update-DR.
. Measure DR chain length by shifting a known marker and counting the delay.
a. Verify length equals the sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths.
. Verify all three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR and instrument data is captured correctly per the SIB model.
. Security gating:
a. Assert `dbg_disable_i.{dft_secure,dft_nonsecure,dfd} = 1`.
b. Re-shift the all-ON pattern; Update-DR.
c. Verify chain length collapses to SIB bits only and no instrument scan control pulses across the whole gated window.
. Clear the disables; re-shift all-ON and confirm full chain and all scan controls restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_all_on_reset_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-RESET`: every iJTAG host scan output and the extended STAP host scan output is a resolved 0 before the first scan
|`sib_all_on_len_chk` |Full chain length matches sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument widths
|`sib_all_on_ctrl_chk` |All three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR
|`sib_all_on_gate_chk` |Asserted SIB disables force every SIB closed; chain collapses despite all-ON request
|`sib_all_on_restore_chk` |Full chain and scan controls restored after the disables clear
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The all-ON chain reaches full length with every scan control active, collapses to SIB bits when the SIB disables are asserted, and recovers when they clear, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-53-dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test]]
===== 5.3 `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: Exhaustive/random SIB patterns

*Test Coverage*:

* Every combination of closed, open, and gated SIBs walked exhaustively, the 8 on/off patterns (sec_dft x dft x dfd) with every disable clear among them, closing `cg_ijtag_sib` `cp_pattern` and `x_gating` in every pass
* Predicted chain length matches sum of (SIB bit {plus} instrument stub width when SIB ON and ungated, else SIB bit only); the stubs are 4, 5, 6 bits wide behind sec_dft, dft, dfd, so every open subset has a distinct length
* Only enabled, ungated instruments receive their host scan-control assertions
* Randomized per-SIB disables (`dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, `dfd`) force gated SIBs closed regardless of scan pattern
* Capture/update data tracks the SIB reference model across mixed configurations: a SIB captures its effective state, an open instrument stub captures the value the previous scan latched into it

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Walk every combination of closed, open, and gated SIBs: for each disable mask of `dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, and `dfd` in a seeded order, every open set of the mask's ungated SIBs, with a seeded request bit for each gated SIB (27 combinations):
a. Shift each (sec_dft, dft, dfd) pattern composed over the current chain, with seeded values for the instruments already open; Update-DR.
b. Shift a seeded marker word ahead of the chain image, locate its MSB in the TDO stream, and verify the latency equals SIB bits plus the widths of the open instruments.
c. Under a temporal window over that scan, verify `select` pulses only for the open instruments and a gated SIB's controls never pulse.
. For 16 additional randomized iterations:
a. Drive a random per-SIB disable mask and a random SIB pattern; Update-DR.
b. Verify gated SIBs (those with their `dbg_disable_i` field asserted) stay closed despite the requested pattern.
c. Verify chain length accounts for forced-closed SIBs and only non-gated enabled instruments receive scan control.
. Compare the captured bits of every scan against the OCAH-local SIB model for each configuration.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_random_len_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-LEN`: the marker latency through the SELECT_IJTAG chain matches the predicted SIB-bit {plus} ungated-instrument-width sum
|`sib_random_ctrl_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: `select` pulses only for enabled, ungated instruments; a closed SIB's `select` never pulses
|`sib_random_gate_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN` and `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: a gated SIB's `select`/`shift_en`/`capture_en`/`update_en` never pulse and it captures closed under random disable masks
|`sib_random_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the captured SIB states and instrument registers, and the marker passthrough, match the SIB reference model on every scan
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every closed, open, and gated SIB combination plus the randomized security combinations yield correct chain lengths, scan-control assertions, and gating behavior matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-54-dtp_ijtag_dft_test]]
===== 5.4 `dtp_ijtag_dft_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFT secure/non-secure access

*Test Coverage*:

* Independent access to the secure and non-secure DFT instruments via their SIBs (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* Both DFT SIBs may be open simultaneously (no hardware mutual exclusion); chain length sums both instruments
* `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure = 1` gates the non-secure DFT SIB; `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure = 1` gates the secure DFT SIB
* Gating one DFT SIB leaves the other accessible (cross-resource isolation), a gated Update-DR does not modify stored SIB state, and access resumes without TAP reset once the disable clears

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A); enable the non-secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the non-secure chain.
. Disable non-secure, enable secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the secure chain.
. Enable both DFT SIBs; Update-DR; verify both scan controls assert and chain length equals the sum of both instruments.
. Secure gating: assert `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure=1`; verify the secure DFT SIB forced closed (its scan controls never pulse across the gated window) while the non-secure SIB remains openable; clear.
. Non-secure gating: assert `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure=1`; verify the non-secure DFT SIB forced closed while the secure SIB remains openable; clear.
. Stored-state evidence: attempt to open a gated SIB, release the disable without reset, verify the gated open attempt did not stick, then confirm a sanctioned open succeeds.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`dft_access_chk` |Secure and non-secure DFT chains each accessible via their SIB with correct scan controls
|`dft_parallel_chk` |Both DFT SIBs open simultaneously; chain length sums both instruments
|`dft_nonsecure_gate_chk` |Non-secure DFT SIB forced closed under `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure`
|`dft_secure_gate_chk` |Secure DFT SIB forced closed under `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure`
|`dft_isolation_chk` |Gating one DFT SIB leaves the other accessible
|`dft_gate_restore_chk` |Both DFT SIBs restored, without TAP reset, after their disables clear
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Secure and non-secure DFT instruments are independently reachable in the same scan flow, each gated by exactly its own disable field and restored when it clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-55-dtp_ijtag_dfd_test]]
===== 5.5 `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFD access

*Test Coverage*:

* DFD instrument reachable through the iJTAG network when its SIB is open (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts shift control and data shifts correctly through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` / `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i`
* `dbg_disable_i.dfd = 1` forces the DFD SIB closed (gated), restored when the disable clears

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`; open only the DFD SIB; Update-DR.
. Verify `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts; shift a known pattern out on `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` and read it back through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i` via the OCAH-local SIB model.
. Assert `dbg_disable_i.dfd = 1`; attempt to open the DFD SIB.
a. Verify the SIB stays closed and none of the DFD scan controls pulse across the whole gated window.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify the gated open attempt did not stick, then re-open the DFD SIB and verify DFD access is restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`dfd_access_chk` |DFD scan data shifts correctly when the SIB is open and ungated
|`dfd_gate_chk` |DFD SIB forced closed and its scan controls never pulse while `dbg_disable_i.dfd` is asserted
|`dfd_stored_chk` |A gated Update-DR does not modify stored SIB state (no delayed open after release)
|`dfd_restore_chk` |DFD access resumes, without TAP reset, after the disable clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: DFD is reachable when ungated and forced closed when `dbg_disable_i.dfd` is asserted, recovering without reset when the disable clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-56-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test]]
===== 5.6 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (primary TAP master and the downstream slave TAPs behind every STAP host port), OCAH-local STAP chain model

*Objective*: Downstream (I/O) STAP selection proved end to end against a downstream TAP

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the I/O STAP by writing 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1` through composed full-chain scans (the `TAP_3DCR` data register is the 2-bit PTAP 3DCR followed serially by the STAP SIB chain); a selected I/O STAP additionally splices one extra flop into the chain through its TDI lockup latch; every scan issued while a STAP is selected is composed over the full network, IR scans included, because the PTAP routes its instruction shift-out into the STAP chain
* A behavioral downstream TAP (`ocah_jtag_vip` slave device: 5-bit IR, distinct IDCODE, one writable `DS_TDR` of a distinct width) is spliced behind every STAP host port; its IR and selected data register become chain segments the reference model predicts independently of the device engine
* While selected, the downstream IDCODE reads back through the STAP, a network-wide IR scan selects `DS_TDR`, and a per-pass random value written through the chain reads back and is confirmed by the device's Update-DR record; the I/O host port forwards (`tdo_oen` pulses, `tms` follows the live TMS) as corroboration
* `dbg_disable_i.stap_io = 1` stops forwarding, parks the host `tms` at the stored `tms_hold=1` so the downstream TAP sits in Test-Logic-Reset, blocks a 3DCR update attempted while gated, and leaves the downstream register unchanged with no Update-DR recorded
* Once the disable clears, without any reset, the stored selection resumes: the downstream answers IDCODE again (park reset proven) and reloading `DS_TDR` returns the pre-gate value; an unrelated STAP's downstream TAP completes a full load/write/read cycle while the target stays gated and records no update; a fresh configuration recovers fully

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the TAP_3DCR configuration chain (TRST clears it, and it holds through the over-length all-zero scan that follows), then configure the I/O STAP with composed scans: set the PTAP 3DCR `select=1, config_hold=1` in a scan of its own, open its SIB, then write its 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`.
. Run a state-maintaining chain scan inside a temporal window; verify the host `tdo_oen` pulses, host `tms` follows the live TMS, and the captured chain matches the reference model.
. Read the downstream IDCODE through the chain (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`); select `DS_TDR` with a composed IR scan; write a per-pass random value; verify the device recorded exactly one Update-DR with that value (`CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`); read it back through the chain (`CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`).
. Assert `dbg_disable_i.stap_io = 1` and attempt a randomized deselecting 3DCR write:
a. Verify `tdo_oen` never pulses, `tms` parks at the stored `tms_hold=1`, and the host `trst_n` stays deasserted at every edge of the gated window, with no assertion from the disable through the end of the gated scan.
b. Verify the chain readback shows the update was ignored (the STAP captures its masked `stap_sel` as 0 while the stored payload survives).
c. Verify the downstream device recorded no Update-DR, still holds the written value (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`), and has moved from Run-Test/Idle, its state when the disable is asserted, to Test-Logic-Reset (`CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`).
. Clear the disable without reset; verify forwarding resumes from the stored selection, the downstream answers IDCODE again, and reloading `DS_TDR` reads back the pre-gate value (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`).
. Re-assert the disable and configure a neighbor STAP; verify the neighbor forwards while the gated port stays quiet, the neighbor's downstream TAP completes a load/write/read cycle, and the gated port's device records no update (isolation).
. Clear all disables, flush (TRST, which resets the downstream TAPs to IDCODE, then a TAP_3DCR load and an over-length all-zero scan), and configure fresh; verify full recovery with a new `DS_TDR` write and readback.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_ds_select_chk` |Selected I/O STAP forwards: `tdo_oen` pulses and `tms` follows the live TMS (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`)
|`stap_ds_readback_chk` |Composed chain readback matches the reference model in every state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`stap_ds_downstream_chk` |Downstream IDCODE and `DS_TDR` read back through the selected STAP; the device's Update-DR record matches the write (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`)
|`stap_ds_gate_chk` |With `dbg_disable_i.stap_io` asserted, forwarding stops, a gated 3DCR update is ignored, and the downstream TAP moves from Run-Test/Idle into Test-Logic-Reset through the parked TMS with its register frozen (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`)
|`stap_ds_isolation_chk` |An unrelated STAP and its downstream TAP stay usable while the I/O STAP is gated
|`stap_ds_recover_chk` |Selection resumes without reset after release with the pre-gate downstream value intact (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`), and a fresh configuration fully recovers
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The I/O STAP forwards only while selected and enabled, the downstream TAP behind it is programmable and readable exactly then, `dbg_disable_i.stap_io` alone gates it with downstream state preserved and no delayed effect, and recovery needs no reset, with every required `CHK-*` ID recorded and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

b. Verify the chain readback shows the update was ignored (the STAP captures its masked `stap_sel` as 0 while the stored payload survives).

. Clear the disable without reset; verify forwarding resumes from the stored selection, the downstream answers IDCODE again, and reloading `DS_TDR` reads back the pre-gate value (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`).
. Re-assert the disable and configure a neighbor STAP; verify the neighbor forwards while the gated port stays quiet, the neighbor's downstream TAP completes a load/write/read cycle, and the gated port's device records no update (isolation).
. Clear all disables, flush (TRST, which resets the downstream TAPs to IDCODE, then a TAP_3DCR load and an over-length all-zero scan), and configure fresh; verify full recovery with a new `DS_TDR` write and readback.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_smc_select_chk` |Selected SMC debug STAP forwards: `tdo_oen` pulses and `tms` follows the live TMS (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`)
|`stap_smc_readback_chk` |Composed chain readback matches the reference model in every state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`stap_smc_downstream_chk` |Downstream IDCODE and `DS_TDR` read back through the selected STAP; the device's Update-DR record matches the write (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`)

|`stap_smc_isolation_chk` |An unrelated STAP and its downstream TAP stay usable while the SMC debug STAP is gated

'''''
