[[sec-59-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test]]
===== 5.9 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (primary TAP master and the downstream slave TAPs behind every STAP host port), OCAH-local STAP chain model

*Objective*: SEP STAP selection proved end to end against a downstream TAP

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the SEP debug STAP by writing 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1` through composed full-chain scans (the `TAP_3DCR` data register is the 2-bit PTAP 3DCR followed serially by the STAP SIB chain); every scan issued while a STAP is selected is composed over the full network, IR scans included, because the PTAP routes its instruction shift-out into the STAP chain
* A behavioral downstream TAP (`ocah_jtag_vip` slave device: 5-bit IR, distinct IDCODE, one writable `DS_TDR` of a distinct width) is spliced behind every STAP host port; its IR and selected data register become chain segments the reference model predicts independently of the device engine
* While selected, the downstream IDCODE reads back through the STAP, a network-wide IR scan selects `DS_TDR`, and a per-pass random value written through the chain reads back and is confirmed by the device's Update-DR record; the SEP host port forwards (`tdo_oen` pulses, `tms` follows the live TMS) as corroboration
* `dbg_disable_i.stap_sep = 1` stops forwarding, parks the host `tms` at the stored `tms_hold=1` so the downstream TAP sits in Test-Logic-Reset, blocks a 3DCR update attempted while gated, and leaves the downstream register unchanged with no Update-DR recorded
* Once the disable clears, without any reset, the stored selection resumes: the downstream answers IDCODE again (park reset proven) and reloading `DS_TDR` returns the pre-gate value; an unrelated STAP's downstream TAP completes a full load/write/read cycle while the target stays gated and records no update; a fresh configuration recovers fully

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the TAP_3DCR configuration chain (TRST clears it, and it holds through the over-length all-zero scan that follows), then configure the SEP debug STAP with composed scans: set the PTAP 3DCR `select=1, config_hold=1` in a scan of its own, open its SIB, then write its 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`.
. Run a state-maintaining chain scan inside a temporal window; verify the host `tdo_oen` pulses, host `tms` follows the live TMS, and the captured chain matches the reference model.
. Read the downstream IDCODE through the chain (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`); select `DS_TDR` with a composed IR scan; write a per-pass random value; verify the device recorded exactly one Update-DR with that value (`CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`); read it back through the chain (`CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`).
. Assert `dbg_disable_i.stap_sep = 1` and attempt a randomized deselecting 3DCR write:
a. Verify `tdo_oen` never pulses, `tms` parks at the stored `tms_hold=1`, and the host `trst_n` stays deasserted at every edge of the gated window, with no assertion from the disable through the end of the gated scan.
b. Verify the chain readback shows the update was ignored (the STAP captures its masked `stap_sel` as 0 while the stored payload survives).
c. Verify the downstream device recorded no Update-DR, still holds the written value (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`), and has moved from Run-Test/Idle, its state when the disable is asserted, to Test-Logic-Reset (`CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`).
. Clear the disable without reset; verify forwarding resumes from the stored selection, the downstream answers IDCODE again, and reloading `DS_TDR` reads back the pre-gate value (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`).
. Re-assert the disable and configure a neighbor STAP; verify the neighbor forwards while the gated port stays quiet, the neighbor's downstream TAP completes a load/write/read cycle, and the gated port's device records no update (isolation).
. Clear all disables, flush (TRST, which resets the downstream TAPs to IDCODE, then a TAP_3DCR load and an over-length all-zero scan), and configure fresh; verify full recovery with a new `DS_TDR` write and readback.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_sep_select_chk` |Selected SEP debug STAP forwards: `tdo_oen` pulses and `tms` follows the live TMS (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`)
|`stap_sep_readback_chk` |Composed chain readback matches the reference model in every state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`stap_sep_downstream_chk` |Downstream IDCODE and `DS_TDR` read back through the selected STAP; the device's Update-DR record matches the write (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`)
|`stap_sep_gate_chk` |With `dbg_disable_i.stap_sep` asserted, forwarding stops, a gated 3DCR update is ignored, and the downstream TAP moves from Run-Test/Idle into Test-Logic-Reset through the parked TMS with its register frozen (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`)
|`stap_sep_isolation_chk` |An unrelated STAP and its downstream TAP stay usable while the SEP debug STAP is gated
|`stap_sep_recover_chk` |Selection resumes without reset after release with the pre-gate downstream value intact (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`), and a fresh configuration fully recovers
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The SEP debug STAP forwards only while selected and enabled, the downstream TAP behind it is programmable and readable exactly then, `dbg_disable_i.stap_sep` alone gates it with downstream state preserved and no delayed effect, and recovery needs no reset, with every required `CHK-*` ID recorded and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-510-dtp_ext_stap_scan_test]]
===== 5.10 `dtp_ext_stap_scan_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model, the 7-bit host segment in `tb_top`

*Objective*: Extended STAP scan

*Test Coverage*:

* Extended STAP scan interface (`+jtag_stap_host_scan_*+`) enabled via PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1
* For this scenario `tb_top` places a 7-bit host segment, a scan register that captures its own update register, between `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o` and `jtag_stap_host_scan_in_i`; the STAP chain model predicts it at the TDO end of the TAP_3DCR chain, so the captured bits and the position of a marker shifted ahead of the chain image show which return path the PTAP used
* `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` pulses all four scan controls during DR operations, and a seeded value written into the segment through a composed TAP_3DCR scan reads back through `jtag_stap_host_scan_in_i`
* Clearing PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 removes the STAP chain from the scan path: all four host scan controls stay quiet across a DR scan, and TDO carries the PTAP 3DCR
* `dbg_disable_i.stap_host = 1` forces all four `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` signals quiet across the whole gated window (temporal-window evidence) and bypasses scan-in: the PTAP chain return is `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o`, so the marker arrives right behind the internal chain and the segment keeps its value
* Normal scan operation resumes, without any reset, after the disable clears: the segment is back in the return path with its pre-gate value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the TAP_3DCR configuration chain, write PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1 in a scan of its own, then a seeded non-zero segment value in a composed scan via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Inside a temporal window, run a maintain scan with a seeded marker ahead of the chain image; verify all four host scan controls pulse, the captured chain (segment included) matches the reference model, and the marker arrives right behind the chain.
. Clear PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 with a composed scan and run a maintain scan under a temporal window: none of the four host scan controls pulses, TDO carries the PTAP 3DCR, and the marker arrives two bits later.
. Security gating:
a. Re-enable scan (PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1 with a seeded CONFIG_HOLD) with a composed scan, then assert `dbg_disable_i.stap_host = 1`.
b. Inside a temporal window covering the whole gated maintain scan, verify none of the four host scan-control signals pulses and scan-in is bypassed: the captured chain and the marker position match the internal chain alone.
c. Clear the disable without reset and verify the four host scan controls pulse again, the segment reads back its pre-gate value, and the marker arrives behind the full chain.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ext_stap_enable_chk` |The four host scan controls pulse during DR operations when PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1 (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`), and the segment value and the marker return through `jtag_stap_host_scan_in_i` (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`ext_stap_deselect_chk` |With PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 all four host scan controls stay quiet across a DR scan (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`) and TDO carries the PTAP 3DCR with the marker two bits behind it (`CHK-SCAN-OBS`)
|`ext_stap_gate_chk` |With `dbg_disable_i.stap_host` asserted, all four host scan controls stay quiet across the gated window (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`) and scan-in is bypassed: the chain readback and the marker position match the internal chain alone (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`ext_stap_restore_chk` |Normal STAP host scan resumes, without reset, after the disable clears: the four host scan controls pulse and the segment reads back its pre-gate value (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The extended STAP scan controls pulse when selected, stay quiet when deselected, are suppressed while `dbg_disable_i.stap_host` is asserted with the PTAP chain return taken from `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o`, and recover without reset with the segment value intact when it clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-511-dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test]]
===== 5.11 `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: 3DCR config hold

*Test Coverage*:

* CONFIG_HOLD in both PTAP_3DCR and STAP 3DCR protects register contents across Test-Logic-Reset (TLR)
* With CONFIG_HOLD=1, SELECT (PTAP) and SELECT/TMS_HOLD (STAP) survive a forced TLR; TRST clears them at either CONFIG_HOLD value
* With CONFIG_HOLD=0, a TLR clears the affected 3DCR fields to 0
* Configuration values are exercised through composed TAP_3DCR chain scans (`OcahJtagMasterDriver`) against the STAP reference model, for every STAP in a seeded order, with a seeded STAP SELECT so the retained selection is proved spliced and unspliced
* `cg_3dcr_hold` `x_hold`: the PTAP 3DCR and every STAP 3DCR through Test-Logic-Reset and TRST at both CONFIG_HOLD values

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the chain; write PTAP_3DCR with CONFIG_HOLD=h, SELECT=1 in a scan of its own, then open the STAP's SIB; Update-DR.
. Write the STAP 3DCR with CONFIG_HOLD=h, a seeded SELECT, TMS_HOLD=1; Update-DR.
. Force TLR (5x TMS=1), or TRST for the TRST sub-case; the IR returns to IDCODE.
. Reload TAP_3DCR with a composed IR scan (while the PTAP SELECT is set, every IR scan shifts through the STAP chain).
a. With the PTAP SELECT retained (h=1 across TLR), a maintain scan returns the chain: verify the captured PTAP SELECT=1 and CONFIG_HOLD=1.
b. With the PTAP SELECT cleared (h=0, or TRST), TDO carries the PTAP 3DCR itself: verify both bits read 0 and that a marker shifted behind them arrives two bits later (the chain return would delay it by the chain length).
. Re-select the PTAP and reopen the STAP's SIB; a maintain scan then carries the STAP 3DCR.
a. h=1 across TLR: verify SELECT and TMS_HOLD read back as written.
b. h=0 across TLR, or TRST: verify every STAP 3DCR field reads 0.
. Repeat the four sub-cases (h=1+TLR, h=0+TLR, h=1+TRST, h=0+TRST) in a seeded order for every STAP.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`config_hold_preserve_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: with CONFIG_HOLD=1 the maintain scans after TLR capture PTAP SELECT=1/CONFIG_HOLD=1 and the STAP's written SELECT/TMS_HOLD
|`config_hold_clear_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-OBS` and `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: with CONFIG_HOLD=0 the PTAP 3DCR reads 0 over the TDR return path after TLR (marker position proves the path) and the reopened STAP 3DCR captures 0; TRST clears both regardless of CONFIG_HOLD
|`config_hold_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: every chain readback (PTAP 3DCR, SIB, STAP 3DCR fields) matches the STAP reference model's predicted capture after each reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: CONFIG_HOLD=1 holds both 3DCRs across TLR while CONFIG_HOLD=0 allows TLR to clear them, matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-512-dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test]]
===== 5.12 `dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: 3DCR TMS hold

*Test Coverage*:

* TMS_HOLD field in the STAP 3DCR sets the parked TMS value driven to a deselected STAP
* TMS_HOLD=1 parks the deselected STAP TMS high (drives toward TLR); TMS_HOLD=0 parks it low (drives toward RTI)
* Parked value is observed on the STAP TAP control output (`jtag_stap_<x>_tms`) for a whole maintain scan while the STAP is deselected (SELECT=0); the deselected port never drives `tdo_oen`
* Both TMS_HOLD polarities are exercised for every STAP, in a seeded order, through composed TAP_3DCR chain scans (`OcahJtagMasterDriver`)
* Every 3DCR payload (SELECT, TMS_HOLD, CONFIG_HOLD) on all four STAPs at once, enabled and under the four STAP disables: an enabled port forwards while its SELECT is set and otherwise parks at its TMS_HOLD, a disabled port parks at its stored TMS_HOLD whatever its SELECT, and every chain readback matches the reference model, closing `cg_stap_3dcr` `x_config`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the chain, set the PTAP SELECT in a scan of its own, then open the STAP's SIB; Update-DR.
. Select the STAP via its 3DCR (SELECT=1, TMS_HOLD=h); Update-DR.
. Under a temporal window over a maintain scan while the STAP is selected, count `jtag_stap_<x>_tms` and `jtag_stap_<x>_tdo_oen` high samples per TCK cycle: `tdo_oen` pulses, and the host TMS toggles and equals the primary TMS at every rising TCK edge. This is the positive control for the parked checks that follow, on the same port and the same window shape.
. Deselect the STAP (SELECT=0, TMS_HOLD=h); Update-DR.
. Under a temporal window over a maintain scan, count `jtag_stap_<x>_tms` and `jtag_stap_<x>_tdo_oen` high samples per TCK cycle.
a. h=1: TMS is high on every cycle (parked high toward TLR).
b. h=0: TMS is never high (parked low toward RTI).
c. `tdo_oen` never pulses.
. Compare the maintain scan's captured chain (SIB, SELECT=0, TMS_HOLD=h) against the STAP reference model.
. Repeat for h in {1, 0} in a seeded order and for every STAP in a seeded order.
. Flush the chain, set the PTAP SELECT in a scan of its own, and open all four SIBs; then for eight payload rounds in a seeded order, round r writing payload (r + i) mod 8 to the i-th STAP in one composed scan:
a. Under a temporal window over a maintain scan, verify each port forwards when its SELECT is set and otherwise parks at its TMS_HOLD with `tdo_oen` quiet, and the captured chain matches the reference model.
b. Assert the four STAP disables; under a temporal window over a maintain scan, verify every port parks at its stored TMS_HOLD with `tdo_oen` quiet, and the captured chain matches the reference model; clear the disables.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`tms_hold_selected_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: while the STAP is selected its `tdo_oen` pulses and its TMS follows the live TMS across the maintain scan (positive control)
|`tms_hold_high_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: with TMS_HOLD=1 the deselected STAP's TMS count equals the window's TCK count and `tdo_oen` stays quiet
|`tms_hold_low_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: with TMS_HOLD=0 the deselected STAP's TMS count is 0 and `tdo_oen` stays quiet
|`tms_hold_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the maintain scan's captured 3DCR (SELECT=0, TMS_HOLD=h) matches the STAP reference model, which also supplies the expected parked polarity
|`tms_hold_sweep_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN` and `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: in every payload round each port forwards or parks as its stored SELECT, TMS_HOLD, and disable call for, and both chain readbacks match the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: A selected STAP forwards, and once deselected it parks its TMS at the configured TMS_HOLD value for both polarities, and every 3DCR payload forwards or parks as its fields and the STAP disables call for, matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-513-dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test]]
===== 5.13 `dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB and STAP chain models

*Objective*: Debug-disable matrix over the eight scan-side gate fields

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic one-hot rows for `stap_io`, `stap_smc`, `stap_sep`, `stap_extra`, `stap_host`, `dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, and `dfd`, plus the all-clear and all-disabled boundary masks and seeded multi-hot masks
* Per row: each iJTAG SIB requested open follows its disable (temporal window: a gated SIB's four scan controls never pulse, an effective SIB's select is seen high); each STAP configured through composed TAP_3DCR scans follows its disable (a gated port never forwards and its gated 3DCR write is ignored, verified by chain readback); the extended host scan interface follows `stap_host`
* After the all-disabled row: releasing every disable without reset shows no gated open attempt stuck (release without replay), and a full all-clear row recovers
* Mixed rows prove unrelated-resource isolation; in cocotb every checker-verified outcome samples its `DtpDbgDisableFcov` ledger cell, and the test requires all sixteen scan-side cells and emits the JSON coverage artifact; in both realizations `dtp_scan_fcov` records the same cells, the mask class, isolation, release, and recovery at the signal level in `cg_dbg_disable_scan`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each row mask (all-clear, one-hot per field, seeded multi-hot, all-disabled last): drive the full disable vector and settle four toggling TCK cycles.
. iJTAG evidence: request all three SIBs open inside a temporal window and check each SIB's allowed/blocked outcome against the reference model.
. STAP evidence: with every disable clear, flush the TAP_3DCR chain, set the PTAP SELECT in a scan of its own, open all four SIBs and write all four 3DCR payloads in composed scans, then assert the row mask and run a windowed maintain scan; check per-port forwarding vs quiet with `tms` parked at the stored `tms_hold=1`, the host scan-control window, and the chain readback; then attempt a clearing 3DCR write under the mask and verify by chain readback that the gated ports ignore it and the ungated ports accept it.
. After the final all-disabled row: clear all disables, observe without reprogramming, and verify no SIB select pulses (no delayed open); then rerun the all-clear row as recovery.
. In cocotb, emit the `DtpDbgDisableFcov` JSON artifact and require all sixteen scan-side cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`scan_matrix_ijtag_chk` |Each SIB's select/window outcome follows exactly its disable field
|`scan_matrix_stap_chk` |Each STAP's forwarding outcome follows exactly its disable field; gated 3DCR writes are ignored
|`scan_matrix_host_chk` |The extended host scan controls follow `stap_host`
|`scan_matrix_readback_chk` |Composed chain readbacks match the reference model under every mask
|`scan_matrix_noreplay_chk` |No gated open attempt sticks after the disables release
|`scan_matrix_recovery_chk` |The all-clear row fully recovers after the all-disabled row
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every row mask produces the per-field allowed/blocked outcomes with temporal-window evidence, no delayed effects, and full recovery, closing all sixteen scan-side cells of `DtpDbgDisableFcov` and `cg_dbg_disable_scan`, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-514-dtp_stap_chain_hold_test]]
===== 5.14 `dtp_stap_chain_hold_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: STAP chain holds while the PTAP 3DCR SELECT is clear

*Test Coverage*:

* With PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 the STAP chain's select, capture, shift and update strobes stay low through IR and DR scans under any instruction, so no STAP SIB or 3DCR changes
* All-ones data, which would open every SIB and select every STAP were the chain clocked, is shifted under IDCODE and BYPASS in DR scans of 3, 4, 8 and a seeded 9 to 32 bits
* Once SELECT is set in a TAP_3DCR scan of its own, a composed chain readback shows every SIB closed

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the TAP_3DCR configuration chain (TRST clears it).
. Under one temporal window over the host scan controls and every STAP's `tdo_oen` and `tms`: load IDCODE and shift all-ones DR scans of each width; load BYPASS with an all-ones IR scan and shift the same DR scans.
. Load TAP_3DCR, set PTAP_3DCR SELECT=1 in a scan of its own, then run a maintain scan and compare the captured chain against the STAP reference model.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_chain_hold_quiet_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: no host scan control pulses, no STAP `tdo_oen` pulses, and every STAP `tms` stays at its reset TMS_HOLD of 0 across the unselected scans
|`stap_chain_hold_readback_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the maintain scan captures every SIB closed, matching the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Scans with the PTAP 3DCR SELECT clear leave the STAP chain untouched, and the chain reads back closed once SELECT is set, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-6-xtrig-ctp-and-ctm-scenarios]]
==== 6. XTRIG, CTP, and CTM Scenarios

NOTE: Every scenario in this section ends each pass with `spill_contract_all`
(`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`): since the last system reset, every CSR port spill register
(<<sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test>>) has kept its input READY high exactly while it
holds fewer than two beats and its output VALID high exactly while it holds a beat
(`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` reads 0).

[[sec-61-dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test]]
===== 6.1 `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP wire-OR

*Test Coverage*:

* CTP wire-OR mode (CONFIG.MODE=0) pulse synchronization with configurable pulse stretching
* Output pulse width equals (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clock cycles for a wide multiplier, the single-cycle minimum, and a seeded width in between
* A chiplet's pull of the CTP's shared CT_Req_out wire, at either INVERT sense, synchronized into the `clk_i` domain and delivered to the CTM destination: the port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after the wire is pulled (two synchronizer stages and the registered output) and never when the wire is released
* STATUS.BUSY reads 1 over the CSR path while a pulse of at least nine cycles (STRETCH_MULT >= 8, the CSR read latency bound) is active and 0 once it ends; the registered BUSY tracks the output enable cycle for cycle at every stretch
* Internal cross-trigger pulse delivered to the CTM destination on an external trigger

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure a seeded CTP via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`: CONFIG.MODE=0 (Wire-OR), CONFIG.INVERT=0, STRETCH_MULT=0x000F (16 clocks).
. Drive a single-cycle internal cross-trigger request to the CTP (via CTM routing).
a. Observe CT_Req_out on the CTP BFM; verify its enable asserts and remains asserted for exactly (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clock cycles.
b. Measure the registered BUSY flop alongside the enable: the same width and the same first-rise cycle.
c. Once the enable has risen and STRETCH_MULT >= 8, read CTP STATUS while the pulse is active and verify BUSY=1; after the pulse ends read STATUS and the busy flop and verify both are 0.
. For each INVERT sense, rest the CTP's shared wire at the pull of the board built for that sense (high for INVERT=0, low for INVERT=1), program the sense, route the CTP to the CTM destination, and let the chiplet on the wire pull it for two clocks.
a. Verify the port's `ct_dst` rises exactly three clocks after the pull and once only, and that the internal cross-trigger pulse is delivered to the programmed CTM destination.
. Reprogram STRETCH_MULT=0x0000 (1 clock) and a seeded value 1..14, repeat the internal trigger, and verify the output width and the BUSY behavior for each.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_wire_or_csr_chk` |CONFIG/STRETCH_MULT writes read back the programmed route configuration
|`ctp_wire_or_stretch_chk` |Output enable width equals (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clocks for every multiplier value
|`ctp_wire_or_sync_chk` |A chiplet's pull of the shared wire is synchronized to `clk_i` and delivered to the CTM destination at either INVERT sense
|`ctp_wire_or_receive_chk` |The port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after the wire is pulled and never at the release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|`ctp_wire_or_busy_chk` |STATUS.BUSY reads 1 while a pulse of STRETCH_MULT >= 8 is active and 0 after it ends; the registered BUSY has the enable's width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) and rises in the same cycle for every stretch
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Wire-OR pulse stretching, input synchronization, and BUSY tracking match the reference model for both stretch settings, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-62-dtp_xtrig_p2p_test]]
===== 6.2 `dtp_xtrig_p2p_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP point-to-point

*Test Coverage*:

* CTP point-to-point 4-phase handshake (CONFIG.MODE=1) in both directions
* Request path: CT_Req_out asserts on the trigger, drops on CT_Ack_in, and the port idles once CT_Ack_in deasserts; response path: CT_Ack_out asserts on CT_Req_in and drops once it deasserts
* STATUS reflects each handshake phase (BUSY, REQ_OUT, ACK_IN, REQ_IN, ACK_OUT) and STATUS.BUSY tracks the in-flight handshake on both sides
* Internal cross-trigger delivered to the CTM destination as one pulse on an external trigger

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure a seeded CTP via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`: CONFIG.MODE=1 (Point-to-Point), CONFIG.INVERT=0.
. Drive an internal cross-trigger request to the CTP (via CTM routing).
a. Verify CT_Req_out asserts on the CTP BFM; read STATUS and verify BUSY=1 and REQ_OUT=1 with ACK_IN, REQ_IN, and ACK_OUT 0.
b. Drive CT_Ack_in from the CTP BFM (simulate the external responder); verify CT_Req_out deasserts; read STATUS and verify ACK_IN=1 and BUSY=1 with REQ_OUT, REQ_IN, and ACK_OUT 0.
c. Deassert CT_Ack_in; wait for the busy flop to clear and read STATUS; verify every STATUS field reads 0 and CT_Req_out stays at its idle level from the release through that read.
. Drive CT_Req_in from the CTP BFM (simulate an external trigger).
a. Verify CT_Ack_out asserts and the internal cross-trigger is delivered to the programmed CTM destination as one pulse, with no other internal destination firing; read STATUS and verify REQ_IN=1, ACK_OUT=1, and BUSY=1 with REQ_OUT and ACK_IN 0.
b. Deassert CT_Req_in; verify CT_Ack_out deasserts; wait for the busy flop to clear and read STATUS; verify every STATUS field reads 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_p2p_handshake_chk` |Req/Ack 4-phase handshake completes in both directions at the pad level, each direction starting from an idle pad, and CT_Req_out stays idle once CT_Ack_in deasserts
|`ctp_p2p_fsm_chk` |STATUS reflects each phase in all five fields: REQ_OUT=1, then ACK_IN=1 with REQ_OUT=0 on the request side; REQ_IN=1 with ACK_OUT=1 on the response side; the fields the phase does not raise read 0, and every field reads 0 once the handshake completes
|`ctp_p2p_busy_chk` |STATUS.BUSY is 1 in every in-flight phase of both handshakes and 0 once the acknowledge (request side) or the request (response side) deasserts
|`ctp_p2p_deliver_chk` |The CTM destination is idle before CT_Req_in asserts, then pulses once and returns to idle, and no other internal destination fires
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Both handshake directions complete with correct FSM sequencing, BUSY behavior, and trigger delivery, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-63-dtp_xtrig_reset_test]]
===== 6.3 `dtp_xtrig_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP reset recovery

*Test Coverage*:

* CONFIG.RESET recovery from a P2P handshake deadlock (stalled external ack), with a request the port received held across the reset
* System reset (`rst_n_i`) landing on an active inverted wire-OR pulse clears all CTP register state back to defaults
* No request, acknowledge, or busy activity while either reset holds or across its release
* Normal operation resumes after recovery (a fresh handshake proceeds)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure a seeded CTP in P2P mode and initiate a handshake.
a. Stall the external CT_Ack_in on the CTP BFM to simulate a deadlock.
b. Read STATUS; verify BUSY=1 and REQ_OUT=1 (handshake stuck).
. Recover via CONFIG.RESET.
a. Raise CT_Req_in on the same CTP and verify CT_Ack_out asserts.
b. Write CONFIG.RESET=1 via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
c. Verify the sender is force-cleared and the receiver keeps running: CT_Req_out deasserts, STATUS reads BUSY=1, REQ_OUT=0, REQ_IN=1, and ACK_OUT=1, and CT_Ack_out stays asserted from the write through that read.
d. Release CT_Req_in, verify CT_Ack_out deasserts, and write CONFIG.RESET=1 again; STATUS reads BUSY=0 and REQ_OUT=0, and from then until after CONFIG.RESET=0 is written, CT_Req_out, CT_Ack_out, and the busy flop of the port stay idle.
e. Write CONFIG.RESET=0 to release; verify CTP returns to normal operation and a new handshake proceeds to completion through the route window.
. Program a second CTP as inverted wire-OR with STRETCH_MULT=0xFFFF, route an internal CT to it and to an internal CT output, and pulse the source; verify its output enable is active and the internal CT output pulses. Raise CT_Req_in on the first CTP and hold it once CT_Ack_out asserts.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) while that pulse is active and the request is held, and watch every request/acknowledge output, every CTP busy flop, and every receive pulse from the first held cycle until after release; each of them is active between the second CTP's programming and the reset.
a. Verify CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS of every CTP and every `CT_SRC` select read their reset values after release.
b. Verify zero activity on every watched output and busy flop across the reset window.
. Reprogram a post-reset route on the reset CTP and verify it operates correctly.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_config_reset_chk` |CONFIG.RESET clears a stalled P2P handshake: CT_Req_out deasserts while the CT_Ack_out of a received request stays asserted until CT_Req_in drops (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`), STATUS reads BUSY=0 and REQ_OUT=0 once both sides are idle, and the port stays idle while RESET holds and across its release (`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET`)
|`ctp_sys_reset_chk` |System reset returns CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS of all CTPs and every `CT_SRC` select to their reset values
|`ctp_reset_no_spurious_chk` |The reset window (held cycles through release) records zero activity on every request/acknowledge output, CTP busy flop, and receive pulse, the active stretched pulse included; each watched observable was active before the reset (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`)
|`ctp_reset_recovery_chk` |CTP resumes normal operation: a post-config-reset handshake and a post-system-reset route both match the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: CONFIG.RESET recovers from deadlock, system reset restores defaults with no spurious activity, and post-reset operation matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-64-dtp_xtrig_rand_test]]
===== 6.4 `dtp_xtrig_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTP configuration

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTP configuration across multiple CTP indices, both modes, and varied settings
* MODE, INVERT, and STRETCH_MULT drawn per iteration; the first four iterations of every pass take a point-to-point port at each INVERT and an inverted wire-OR port at STRETCH_MULT 0 and at a seeded 1..7, so every pass covers the inverted `cg_ctp` and `cg_ctp_p2p` bins, `x_mode_inversion_stretch` included
* A point-to-point draw also receives: CT_Req_in at the port's polarity is acknowledged and reaches the internal CT through the reverse route
* INVERT flips the pad polarity in both Wire-OR and P2P modes; the bench drives the request and acknowledge pads at the port's polarity
* STRETCH_MULT respected in Wire-OR mode for each randomized value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Randomly select a CTP index and an internal CT; the first four iterations take (wire-OR, INVERT=1, STRETCH_MULT=0), (P2P, INVERT=0), (wire-OR, INVERT=1, STRETCH_MULT 1..7), and (P2P, INVERT=1), and the rest draw the configuration at random.
b. Randomly choose a legal configuration: MODE (0/1), INVERT (0/1), STRETCH_MULT (0..7, so every pulse drains inside the route window).
c. Write CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`; a P2P draw asserts CONFIG.RESET first so no request latched under a previous mode is pending.
d. Route the internal CT to the CTP and pulse it inside an activity window over every output.
e. Verify the window matches the reference model with no unselected output; wire-OR: the enable pulse width is (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) and the data pad holds the static level INVERT selects; P2P: the request asserts at the port's active level, the acknowledge driven at the port's polarity releases it, and the request returns to its idle level.
f. P2P draws: route the CTP to the internal CT, raise CT_Req_in at the port's active level, verify CT_Ack_out asserts and the internal CT pulses once with no other output firing, release CT_Req_in, and verify CT_Ack_out drops.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_rand_csr_chk` |Random CONFIG/STRETCH_MULT writes read back as programmed
|`ctp_rand_mode_chk` |Wire-OR draws: enable width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) and a quiet unselected set; P2P draws: request asserted, released by the acknowledge, idle again, and a received request acknowledged at the port's polarity and delivered once
|`ctp_rand_invert_chk` |INVERT flips the pad levels: the wire-OR data pad holds the inverted static level while its enable pulses; the P2P request asserts low and idles high with the acknowledge driven low-active
|`ctp_rand_route_chk` |Every draw's output window matches the CTM reference model with no unselected output firing
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized configurations produce reference-model-matching behavior, including INVERT polarity and STRETCH_MULT timing, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-65-dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test]]
===== 6.5 `dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP BFM

*Objective*: CTP CSR deterministic sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic walk of every XTRIG/CTP regblock CSR field (coverage-closure of byte-enable and masked-write condition holes)
* Per-byte byte-enable writes that random selection can miss in the CTP generated regblock
* Reserved-bit / legal-mask behavior and aliasing checks across CTP CSRs
* The hole word of every CTP window, which reads 0 and ignores writes
* Generated routing state produced by each programmed value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle after reset and disable all CTP routing.
. Walk every CTP CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT with deterministic values, in seeded order.
a. Write the field patterns, the all-ones word, and the inverted patterns through `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`, comparing the full readback word so reserved bits must read 0.
b. Apply every byte strobe (0x1, 0x2, 0x4, 0x8) with a seeded word over a seeded nonzero base and compare the merged word; a strobed reserved lane leaves the register unchanged.
c. Write all ones to STATUS and read it back unchanged.
d. After the final words of the next step, write all ones to the hole word past STRETCH_MULT and read 0 back; CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT keep the final words.
. Before each CTP's sweep, program the neighbouring CTP's CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT with seeded nonzero words, end the sweep on CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT words that differ from the neighbour's, and verify afterwards that the neighbour holds those words (no aliasing).
. After the sweep, program one CTP as wire-OR with a seeded STRETCH_MULT, route an internal CT to it, and verify the route window, the reference model, and the pulse width.
. Disable all CTP routing before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_csr_sweep_byteen_chk` |Each byte strobe (0x1, 0x2, 0x4, 0x8) updates only the strobed lanes of CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT; a strobed reserved lane leaves the register unchanged
|`ctp_csr_sweep_readback_chk` |Every full-word pattern reads back its defined fields with the reserved bits 0
|`ctp_csr_sweep_reserved_chk` |All-ones and inverted patterns leave the reserved bits reading 0; a write to STATUS is ignored
|`ctp_csr_sweep_alias_chk` |The neighbouring CTP's CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT are unchanged by the sweep; a routed pulse after the sweep matches the reference model with width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1)
|`ctp_csr_sweep_hole_chk` |The hole word of every window completes the write and the read with OKAY and reads 0, and the write leaves that CTP's CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT on their final words
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every CTP CSR field accepts and returns deterministic values across all byte-enable patterns with correct reserved-bit, hole, and routing behavior and no aliasing, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-66-dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_dst_port_sweep_test]]
===== 6.6 `dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_dst_port_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: Destination-port toggle sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic sweep of every CTP destination-port selection (coverage-closure of destination-port toggles random selection can miss)
* Exactly the programmed destination toggles for a single source trigger (one-hot delivery)
* No unintended destination ports toggle
* Every CTP routed to itself in point-to-point mode, where its request and acknowledge pads differ on the receive and send sides, so the trigger it receives leaves on its own CT_Req_out once

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Disable all CTM/CTP routing and idle the CSR path after reset.
. Sweep each destination port selection deterministically.
a. Program one destination-port mask at a time via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Drive a single source trigger via CTM routing.
c. Observe the CTP/CTM BFM outputs and verify exactly the programmed destination toggles while all others remain idle.
d. Pulse the internal CT acknowledge for that destination; every route of the sweep is wire-OR, where the acknowledge is unused, so the pulse is stimulus only.
. Repeat across all configured destination-port selections.
. Route each CTP to itself in point-to-point mode: program its CT_SRC register with its own input bit, raise its CT_Req_in as the four-phase sender, and verify exactly its CT_Req_out asserts, once, and completes the handshake with the bench while every other output stays idle.
. Compare the full toggle sequence against the reference model and disable routing on exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_csr_chk` |Destination-port masks read back as programmed
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_onehot_chk` |Exactly the programmed destination toggles per source trigger
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_iso_chk` |No unprogrammed destination port toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every destination-port selection toggles exactly its programmed destination with no spurious toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-67-dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test]]
===== 6.7 `dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: XTRIG regblock accepted-access path

*Test Coverage*:

* With one access in flight the XTRIG regblock presents the crossbar a ready interface, so its local stall (back-pressure) paths stay idle: the crossbar demux's AW and AR READY-low stall counters (`dtp_tb_if`), which count the cycles a request waits at the demux behind the CSR port's spill registers (<<sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test>>), do not advance across the accesses. The regblock stalls only with a read and a write to it in flight together (<<sec-630-dtp_xtrig_axi_outstanding_test>>)
* Confirms XTRIG CSR writes and reads complete without corruption when the register interface is exercised
* No trigger state changes occur while an access is in flight: an activity window over the internal-lane requests, every CTP request and acknowledge enable, and every CTP busy flop spans the accesses (the pad levels follow the polarity CSR the accesses write, so they stay out of it, and the internal lanes run in pulse mode, where their acknowledge is unused)
* Two routes after the accesses are the positive control: a wire-OR route from an internal CT to a CTP and a second internal CT, and a point-to-point route from an internal CT to another CTP, move every observable the quiet records judge

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Open an activity window over the internal-lane requests, every CTP request/acknowledge enable, and every CTP busy flop, then exercise representative XTRIG CSR writes and reads through `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` on the accepted-access path.
a. Verify each write/read completes without corruption.
b. Close the window after the last access and verify zero activity across it, then a further quiet window after the accesses.
. Verify the crossbar demux's AW and AR stall counters did not advance across the accesses while the port's AWVALID and ARVALID counters did.
. Program the two control routes and drive them inside an activity window over the same observables: each of them moves.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_reg_stall_ready_chk` |The crossbar demux's AW and AR READY-low stall counters do not advance across the exercised accesses (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_reg_stall_csr_chk` |Accepted XTRIG CSR writes/reads read back without corruption
|`xtrig_reg_stall_no_premature_chk` |No internal-lane request, CTP request or acknowledge enable, or CTP busy flop changes while the accesses are in flight (`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET`), and each of them moves in the control window afterwards (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Accepted XTRIG CSR accesses complete uncorrupted and the regblock never stalls an access issued alone, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test]]
===== 6.8 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Manual AXI-Lite channel skew

*Test Coverage*:

* Manual XTRIG AXI-Lite AW/W/R/B channel skew for demux ready-low and response-hold coverage
* The crossbar registers each of the five channels at the CSR port (`hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_network/doc/architecture.adoc`, Address Map) in a spill register, a two-beat register that keeps its input READY high exactly while it holds fewer than two beats and its output VALID high exactly while it holds a beat, so the port takes a beat that must wait and the crossbar demux behind it holds the beat. The bench checks this rule on all five channels every cycle (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count`). The W-first pair of <<sec-69-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test>> fills the W spill register and the RREADY hold of <<sec-610-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test>> fills the R one; the AW and AR spill registers pass the first beat of each pair to the crossbar demux no later than the cycle they take the second, so a pair leaves at most one beat in them, and the bursts of <<sec-630-dtp_xtrig_axi_outstanding_test>> fill them
* Write-channel skew: AW and W arrival offset in both orders with master-driven valid/ready timing
* Read-channel skew: AR issued with the slave R response held to exercise response-hold paths
* Protocol completion and the stored register value under skew, each skewed access completing with its own response before the next is issued

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the XTRIG AXI-Lite master in manual mode so AW/W/AR/B/R channel timing is driven explicitly.
. Apply write-channel skew.
a. Drive AW before W (and W before AW) with a configurable gap, holding the later channel's VALID low, and hold BREADY low for a seeded 5 to 8 cycles after the request phase, so the port holds each B response; the port's W spill register takes the W-first write's W beat with WREADY high (`xtrig_axil_w_stall_count` does not advance), and the crossbar demux holds the beat until the cycle after its AW enters the demux's W-select queue (`xtrig_demux_w_stall_count` advances by at least the AW delay plus one); every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` does not advance).
b. Verify each write completes with an OKAY B response and the target CSR then holds the written value, each write replacing a different value.
. Apply read-channel skew.
a. Issue AR, then hold the R response by keeping the master RREADY low for several cycles (response-hold).
b. Verify RVALID, RDATA, and RRESP stay stable while RREADY is held low and the read returns the written value when released.
. Verify each skewed access completes with its own response before the next is issued and that the final STRETCH_MULT contents equal the last written value.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_axi_skew_aw_w_chk` |AW/W skew in both orders completes with an OKAY B response, and a readback after each skewed write returns the written value
|`xtrig_axi_skew_ready_low_chk` |The port takes the W-first write's W beat with WREADY high (port W stall counter), the crossbar demux holds it with its W READY low for at least the AW delay plus one cycle (demux W stall counter), every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (spill-register counter), and the R channel holds RVALID, RDATA, and RRESP stable through the response-hold window; on four-state simulators the bench's four-state RDATA-known rule on the XTRIG CSR port holds RDATA resolved while RVALID is high
|`xtrig_axi_skew_order_chk` |Each skewed access completes with its own response before the next is issued
|`xtrig_axi_skew_csr_chk` |Final XTRIG CSR contents match the programmed values
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All skewed AXI-Lite write and read accesses complete with correct responses, stable channels, and matching CSR contents, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-69-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test]]
===== 6.9 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Demux pending-W admission and release

*Test Coverage*:

* Two outstanding skewed writes through the shared AXI-Lite master: the second AW is presented while the first W is still pending
* The demux's AW-before-W bookkeeping, mirrored from the crossbar as `xtrig_demux_w_pending` (the W-select queue holds the first AW's port), engages on the first AW and releases when its W passes; meanwhile the demux holds the second AW and does not accept it, as the demux open-write counters show, and the port stall counter `xtrig_axil_aw_stall_count` and the VIP count the port's AW stall cycles
* The port's spill registers (<<sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test>>) take the pair ahead of the demux: the port accepts the second AW of the AW-first pair while the first is owed its W (`xtrig_axil_aw_open_accept_count` advances by one), and the W spill register holds both W beats of the W-first pair (`xtrig_axil_w_spill_full_count` advances)
* The demux AW lock flag, mirrored as `xtrig_demux_aw_lock`, stays clear: it engages only when the addressed subordinate refuses an AW the demux presents, and the subordinates this traffic addresses accept every AW presented to them
* Response ordering across the pair proven by distinguishable response codes (OKAY then DECERR) and by both readbacks

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the XTRIG AXI-Lite master in manual mode and open an activity window over the two demux mirrors.
. Issue an AW-first pair to two seeded CTP CONFIG registers: the first W delayed 4..8 cycles, BREADY deferred 1..4 cycles.
a. Verify `xtrig_demux_w_pending` was 1 during the pair and is 0 at its end, and `xtrig_demux_aw_lock` stayed 0.
b. Verify the port accepted the second AW while the first write was owed its W (`xtrig_axil_aw_open_accept_count` advanced by one), that the crossbar demux then held it and did not accept it before the first W passed (`xtrig_demux_aw_open_stall_count` advanced, `xtrig_demux_aw_open_accept_count` did not), that every spill register's READY and VALID matched the beats it holds (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` did not advance), and that the port AW stall counter advanced by the VIP's stall count.
c. Verify both B responses are OKAY and both CONFIG readbacks match.
. Issue a W-first pair to a CTM select register and an unmapped word (AW delayed 4..8 cycles).
a. Verify the first B is OKAY and the second DECERR, in issue order, with the same demux-state, demux open-write, spill-register and port stall evidence, and that the W spill register held both W beats (`xtrig_axil_w_spill_full_count` advanced).
b. Verify the CTM select readback matches.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_demux_w_pending_engage_chk` |The first AW of the pair engages the demux's pending-W state (`xtrig_demux_w_pending`=1) and the demux holds the second AW until the first W passes (demux open-write counters)
|`xtrig_demux_w_pending_release_chk` |The pending-W state releases once the first W passes (`xtrig_demux_w_pending`=0 at the end of the pair) and the port AW stall counter equals the VIP's stall count
|`xtrig_demux_aw_lock_clear_chk` |The AW lock flag (`xtrig_demux_aw_lock`) stays 0 through each pair
|`xtrig_demux_port_spill_chk` |The port accepts the second AW of the AW-first pair while the first is owed its W, the W spill register holds both W beats of the W-first pair, and every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_demux_order_chk` |With the pair's second write aimed at an unmapped word, the first B is OKAY and the second DECERR, in issue order, and both responses complete
|`xtrig_demux_csr_chk` |Both CTP CONFIG readbacks and the CTM select readback match the programmed values
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The demux's pending-W state engages on the first AW and releases when its W passes, the second AW waits for it at the demux, the AW lock flag stays clear, responses complete in issue order, and the CSR readbacks match, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-610-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test]]
===== 6.10 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Read decode backpressure

*Test Coverage*:

* An unmapped XTRIG read answered DECERR: no subordinate decodes the address
* Two outstanding reads through the shared AXI-Lite master, a CTP STRETCH_MULT read and then an unmapped read: the second AR is presented while RREADY is held on the first response, the port's AR spill register (<<sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test>>) accepts it, and the crossbar demux, which admits one read in flight, holds it until the first response passes the demux into the port's R spill register; the R spill register then holds both responses during the RREADY hold. The two reads address different subordinates, so the demux alone holds the second AR
* The first read returns OKAY with the STRETCH_MULT value written before the pair and the second DECERR, in issue order; no deadlock on the backpressured read path
* Stable read data (RVALID, RDATA, RRESP) through the decode-backpressure window

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Idle all manual AXI-Lite master outputs after reset; the XTRIG crossbar address map has no subordinate at the unmapped base (the word after the last CTP window) and above.
. Write a seeded nonzero value to the STRETCH_MULT register of a seeded CTP, then issue a read of that register and a read of an unmapped word at a seeded offset as one pair: hold the first response by keeping RREADY=0 for 4..8 cycles while the second AR is presented.
a. Verify the port accepted the second AR while the first read was open (`xtrig_axil_ar_open_accept_count` advanced by one), that the crossbar demux then held it and did not accept it before the first response passed the demux (`xtrig_demux_ar_open_stall_count` advanced, `xtrig_demux_ar_open_accept_count` did not), that the R spill register held both responses during the hold (`xtrig_axil_r_spill_full_count` advanced), that every spill register's READY and VALID matched the beats it holds (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` did not advance), and that the port counter `xtrig_axil_ar_stall_count` advanced by the VIP's stall count.
b. Verify RVALID/RDATA/RRESP stayed stable across the hold.
. Release RREADY, let both responses complete, and verify the first is OKAY with the written STRETCH_MULT value and the second DECERR.
. Verify exactly two AR beats were accepted (ARVALID cycles minus stall cycles).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_read_decerr_chk` |The first read returns OKAY with the written STRETCH_MULT value and the unmapped second read DECERR
|`xtrig_read_ar_held_chk` |The crossbar demux holds the second AR while the first read is open and does not accept it before the first response passes the demux (demux open-read counters), the port AR stall counter equals the VIP's count, and exactly two AR beats are accepted
|`xtrig_read_port_spill_chk` |The port accepts the second AR while the first read is open, its R spill register holds both responses through the RREADY hold, and every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_read_no_deadlock_chk` |Both responses complete once RREADY is released, and RVALID/RDATA/RRESP hold stable across the RREADY-low window; on four-state simulators the bench's four-state RDATA-known rule on the XTRIG CSR port holds RDATA resolved while RVALID is high
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The mapped read returns OKAY with the written value and the unmapped read DECERR, with stable read data through the backpressure window and the second AR held at the demux until the first response passes it, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

NOTE: In the CTM entries that follow, `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` is
the register of output port `dst`, and each set bit names a source input port
(see the port-naming note at the head of this section).

[[sec-611-dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.11 `dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Wire-OR CLA to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM wire-OR (multicast) routing of one internal CLA source to multiple external CTP destinations
* `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` of every selected CTP output programmed with the single CLA source-input bit; the overlap step adds a second source-input bit to one of them
* All selected CTP destinations receive the trigger on the same source pulse; non-selected ports stay idle
* Wire-OR superposition when a second overlapping CLA source targets one of the same CTP destinations
* CSR readback of each selected output register matches its programmed source-input bits

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the multicast route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` for each selected CTP output `dst` (e.g. outputs 0, 5, 12).
b. Read each written register back and confirm only the source-input bit is set.
. Drive a cross-trigger request on the CLA source via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the CTP destinations with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify every selected CTP destination asserts the trigger simultaneously.
b. Verify every non-selected destination port remains quiet.
. Exercise wire-OR superposition:
a. Program one CTP output's `CT_DST_SELECT` with the source-input bit and a second CLA source-input bit.
b. Pulse each source alone, then both in the same cycle: the shared destination fires for each, and the two merge into one pulse of the single-source width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1, two cycles) while every other destination stays quiet.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_csr_chk` |CSR readback of each selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` matches its programmed source-input bits
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_route_chk` |All selected CTP destinations assert on the CLA pulse against the reference model
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_or_chk` |Each of the two sources selected on the shared CTP output fires it alone; both pulsed in the same cycle produce one merged pulse of width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1)
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No non-selected destination port toggles
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_receive_chk` |The internal port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after the CLA request rises and never at its release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA source multicasts to exactly its selected CTP destinations, overlapping sources OR correctly, and no spurious destination toggles occur, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-612-dtp_ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.12 `dtp_ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Wire-OR CTP to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM wire-OR routing of one external CTP source to multiple internal CLA destinations
* `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` of every selected CLA output programmed with the single CTP source-input bit
* All selected CLA destinations observe the trigger on the same CTP input pulse; non-selected stay idle
* Wire-OR superposition when an overlapping CTP source targets a shared CLA destination
* CSR readback of each selected output register matches its programmed source-input bits

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the multicast route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` for each selected CLA output `dst`.
b. Read each written register back and confirm only the source-input bit is set.
. Let the chiplet on the source CTP's shared wire pull it via the OCAH-local CTP BFM.
. Observe the CLA destinations with the OCAH-local CTM monitor:
a. Verify every selected CLA destination asserts simultaneously.
b. Verify non-selected CLA destinations remain quiet.
. Exercise wire-OR superposition with a second CTP source selected on one CLA output: pulse each source alone, then both in the same cycle, and confirm the shared CLA destination fires for each and the two merge into one pulse of the single-source width (two cycles, the internal CTP stretch).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_csr_chk` |CSR readback of each selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` matches its programmed source-input bits
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_route_chk` |All selected CLA destinations assert on the CTP pulse against the reference model
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_or_chk` |Each of the two sources selected on the shared CLA output fires it alone; both pulsed in the same cycle produce one merged two-cycle pulse
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CLA destination toggles
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_receive_chk` |The source port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after its wire is pulled and never at the release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CTP source multicasts to exactly its selected CLA destinations, overlapping sources OR correctly, and no spurious destination toggles occur, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-613-dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.13 `dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Wire-OR CLA to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM wire-OR routing entirely between internal CLA channels (source CLA to multiple destination CLA)
* `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` of every selected CLA output programmed with the single CLA source-input bit
* All selected destination CLA channels pulse on the source CLA pulse; non-selected stay idle
* Wire-OR superposition of two CLA sources sharing a destination CLA channel
* No feedback loop where a destination CLA re-triggers its own source route

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the intra-CLA multicast route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` for each selected CLA output `dst`.
b. Read each written register back and confirm only the source-input bit is set.
. Pulse the source CLA via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the destination CLA channels with the OCAH-local CTM monitor:
a. Verify every selected destination CLA pulses.
b. Verify the routing mask behavior: non-selected CLA channels stay idle and the source is not fed back to itself.
. Exercise wire-OR superposition with a second CLA source selected on one destination channel: pulse each source alone, then both in the same cycle, and confirm the shared channel fires for each and the two merge into one pulse of the single-source width (two cycles, the internal CTP stretch).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_csr_chk` |CSR readback of each selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` matches its programmed source-input bits
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_route_chk` |All selected destination CLA channels pulse against the reference model
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_or_chk` |Each of the two sources selected on the shared CLA channel fires it alone; both pulsed in the same cycle produce one merged two-cycle pulse
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_noloop_chk` |No feedback loop or self-retrigger of the source CLA
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_cla_receive_chk` |The internal port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after the CLA request rises and never at its release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA source multicasts to exactly its selected CLA destinations with correct wire-OR superposition and no feedback or spurious toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-614-dtp_ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.14 `dtp_ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Wire-OR CTP to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM wire-OR routing entirely between external CTP ports (source CTP to multiple destination CTP)
* `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` of every selected CTP output programmed with the single CTP source-input bit
* All selected destination CTP ports pulse with matching pulse width on the source CTP pulse
* Wire-OR superposition of two CTP sources sharing a destination CTP port
* No feedback loop from a destination CTP port back into the source route

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the inter-CTP multicast route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` for each selected CTP output `dst`.
b. Read each written register back and confirm only the source-input bit is set.
. Let the chiplet on the source CTP's shared wire pull it via the OCAH-local CTP BFM.
. Observe the destination CTP ports with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify every selected destination CTP port pulses.
b. Verify pulse width on each destination matches the reference model.
c. Verify no feedback loop re-triggers the source.
. Exercise wire-OR superposition with a second CTP source selected on one destination port: pulse each source alone, then both in the same cycle, and confirm the shared port fires for each and the two merge into one pulse of the single-source width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1, two cycles).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_csr_chk` |CSR readback of each selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` matches its programmed source-input bits
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_route_chk` |All selected destination CTP ports pulse against the reference model
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_width_chk` |Each of the two sources selected on the shared CTP port fires it alone; both pulsed in the same cycle produce one merged pulse of width (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1)
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_noloop_chk` |No feedback loop or self-retrigger of the source CTP
|`ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_ctp_receive_chk` |The source port's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after its wire is pulled and never at the release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CTP source multicasts to exactly its selected CTP destinations with correct pulse width, wire-OR superposition, and no feedback or spurious toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-615-dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.15 `dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: P2P CLA to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM point-to-point routing of an internal CLA source to a single external CTP destination
* `+CT_SRC[ctp_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with exactly one source-input bit set
* Request appears only at the selected CTP destination; no spurious triggers elsewhere
* Several distinct CLA-source / CTP-destination pairs exercised to vary the single-bit position
* The destination CTP holds CT_Req_out while CT_Ack_in is withheld, drops it on CT_Ack_in, and idles once CT_Ack_in deasserts; the bench withholds CT_Ack_in for a seeded 4 to 16 cycles and releases it a seeded 4 to 16 cycles after CT_Req_out drops

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the point-to-point route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[ctp_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` so the destination CTP output selects the single source input.
b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.
. Drive a cross-trigger request on the CLA source via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the destinations with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify the request appears at exactly the selected CTP destination.
b. Verify no other destination port observes the request.
. Repeat for several CLA-source / CTP-destination combinations to move the single set bit across the destination field.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_csr_chk` |CSR readback of the destination CTP output register shows exactly one source-input bit set
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_route_chk` |Trigger reaches exactly the selected CTP destination against the reference model
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers on non-selected destinations
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA-to-CTP trigger is delivered only to the configured destination across all exercised pairs with no spurious activity, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-616-dtp_ctm_p2p_ctp_to_cla_test]]

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

* `+CT_SRC[cla_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with exactly one source-input bit set
* Request appears only at the selected CLA destination; no spurious triggers elsewhere

b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.

b. Verify no other destination observes the request.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description

|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_cla_route_chk` |Trigger reaches exactly the selected CLA destination against the reference model
|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers on non-selected destinations
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CTP-to-CLA trigger is delivered only to the configured destination across all exercised pairs with no spurious activity, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-617-dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.17 `dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: P2P CLA to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM point-to-point routing entirely between internal CLA channels (single source CLA to single destination CLA)
* `+CT_SRC[cla_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with exactly one source-input bit set
* Request appears only at the selected destination CLA; no spurious triggers elsewhere
* No feedback loop where the source CLA re-triggers itself
* The bench configuration runs every internal lane in pulse mode, so the route carries one pulse and involves no handshake

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the intra-CLA point-to-point route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[cla_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` so the destination CLA output selects the single source input.
b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.
. Drive the source CLA request via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the CLA destinations with the OCAH-local CTM monitor:
a. Verify the request reaches exactly the selected destination CLA.
b. Verify no other CLA channel observes the request and the source is not fed back to itself.
. Repeat for several source/destination CLA combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description

|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_route_chk` |Trigger reaches exactly the selected CLA destination against the reference model
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers and no source self-retrigger
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA-to-CLA trigger is delivered only to the configured destination with no spurious activity or feedback, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-618-dtp_ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_test]]

* Request appears only at the selected destination CTP; no spurious triggers elsewhere
* No feedback loop from the destination CTP back into the source route
* The source CTP raises CT_Ack_out on CT_Req_in and drops it once CT_Req_in deasserts; the bench holds CT_Req_in until CT_Ack_out has been asserted for a seeded 4 to 16 cycles
* The destination CTP holds CT_Req_out while CT_Ack_in is withheld, drops it on CT_Ack_in, and idles once CT_Ack_in deasserts; the bench withholds CT_Ack_in for a seeded 4 to 16 cycles and releases it a seeded 4 to 16 cycles after CT_Req_out drops

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the inter-CTP point-to-point route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[ctp_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` so the destination CTP output selects the single source input.
b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.
. Raise CT_Req_in on the source CTP via the OCAH-local CTP BFM, hold it until CT_Ack_out has been asserted for a seeded 4 to 16 cycles, then release it and see CT_Ack_out drop.
. Observe the CTP destinations with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify the request reaches exactly the selected destination CTP.
b. Verify no other CTP port observes the request and no feedback loop re-triggers the source.
. Repeat for several source/destination CTP combinations.

[cols=",",options="header",]

|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers and no source feedback loop
|===
