'''''

[[sec-619-dtp_ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_test]]
===== 6.19 `dtp_ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset in wire-OR mode

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears all CTM wire-OR routing registers to `0x0000_0000` and every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS to its reset value
* No request, internal request, busy, or receive-pulse activity on any port while the reset holds or across its release, the inverted output's request data pad included; wire-OR mode holds the acknowledge pads static, so they are not watched; a wire resting at its pull through the release is not a trigger
* CTM accepts new wire-OR configuration after reset and routes correctly
* Pre-reset multicast routing and the stretched pulses still active when the reset lands do not survive it

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish pre-reset wire-OR state:
a. Program two CTP outputs with STRETCH_MULT=0xFFFF, one of them with CONFIG.INVERT=1, and select one CLA source on their `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0+` registers and on one internal CT output via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Pulse the source and confirm both output enables are active, the internal CT output pulses, each CTP output receives its own pull, and STATUS.BUSY reads 1.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) while the stretched pulses are active and watch every request enable and request data pad, the internal requests, every CTP busy flop, and every receive pulse from the first held cycle until after release; each of them is active between the source pulse and the reset.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`; read every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS and confirm the reset values.
b. Confirm zero activity on every watched output and busy flop across the reset window.
. Reconfigure a fresh wire-OR route to two or three outputs post-reset, pulse the source, and verify correct multicast routing resumes.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` and every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS reads its reset value after reset
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_nospur_chk` |The stretched pulses active when the reset lands are cleared: zero activity on every request enable and request data pad, internal request, CTP busy flop, and port receive pulse while the reset holds and across its release, each of which is active before the reset (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`)
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_route_chk` |Post-reset wire-OR reconfiguration to two or three outputs routes correctly against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset clears all wire-OR routing state with no spurious destination toggles, and post-reset multicast routing matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-620-dtp_ctm_reset_p2p_mode_test]]
===== 6.20 `dtp_ctm_reset_p2p_mode_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset in P2P mode

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears all CTM point-to-point routing registers to `0x0000_0000` and every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS to its reset value
* A point-to-point request still awaiting its acknowledge when the reset lands is cleared: CT_Req_out drops and STATUS reads BUSY=0, REQ_OUT=0
* No request, acknowledge, or busy activity on any destination while the reset holds or across its release
* A post-reset point-to-point transaction completes correctly

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish pre-reset P2P state:
a. Program a CTP output in P2P mode and select one CLA source on its `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0+` via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Pulse the source without driving the acknowledge; confirm CT_Req_out is asserted and STATUS reads BUSY=1, REQ_OUT=1.
c. Program a second CTP in P2P mode, route its receive to an internal CT output, raise its CT_Req_in, and hold it once CT_Ack_out asserts and the internal CT output pulses.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) with the request pending and the received request held, and watch every request/acknowledge output, every CTP busy flop, and every receive pulse from the first held cycle until after release; each of them is active between the source pulse and the reset.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read STATUS and confirm BUSY=0 and REQ_OUT=0; read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`; read every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS and confirm the reset values.
b. Confirm zero activity on every watched output and busy flop across the reset window.
. Reprogram a single-bit P2P route post-reset, drive the source, and verify the transaction completes only at the selected destination.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` and every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS reads its reset value after reset
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_nospur_chk` |The request pending when the reset lands is cleared (CT_Req_out low, STATUS BUSY=0 and REQ_OUT=0 afterwards) with zero activity on every request/acknowledge output, CTP busy flop, and receive pulse across the reset window, each of which is active before the reset (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`)
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_route_chk` |Post-reset point-to-point transaction completes at the selected destination against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset clears all point-to-point routing and handshake state with no spurious activity, and a post-reset P2P transaction completes correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-621-dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test]]
===== 6.21 `dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset across all modes

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears CTM routing state programmed across both wire-OR and point-to-point modes
* All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` return to `0x0000_0000` default regardless of pre-reset mode mix
* No spurious cross-trigger requests on any destination during or after reset
* Both wire-OR and point-to-point routes can be reprogrammed and route correctly post-reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish a mixed pre-reset configuration, programmed together so both classes are live when the reset lands:
a. Select one wire-OR source on two CTP outputs and one internal CT output, and one P2P source on a third CTP output, via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`, without clearing between them.
b. Drive each source and confirm correct routing before reset (the internal CT output is a live observable of this scenario).
c. Leave a P2P request pending without its acknowledge.
d. With a fifth CTP programmed in P2P mode alongside the others, raise its CT_Req_in and hold it once CT_Ack_out asserts.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) with both route classes programmed, the request pending, and the received request held, and watch every request/acknowledge output, every CTP busy flop, and every receive pulse from the first held cycle until after release; each of them is active between the first pre-reset pulse and the reset, and the pending port reads BUSY=0 and REQ_OUT=0 afterwards.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`; read every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS and confirm the reset values.
b. Confirm zero activity on every watched output and busy flop across the reset window.
. Reprogram both a wire-OR and a point-to-point route post-reset, pulse the sources, and verify each routes correctly.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` and every CTP CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT, and STATUS reads its reset value after reset regardless of pre-reset mode
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_nospur_chk` |Zero activity on every request/acknowledge output, CTP busy flop, and receive pulse while the reset holds and across its release, each of which is active before the reset (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`)
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_route_chk` |Post-reset wire-OR and P2P reconfiguration both route correctly against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset returns all routing state to defaults for both modes with no spurious activity, and both wire-OR and P2P routes reprogram correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-622-dtp_ctm_rand_all_scenarios_test]]
===== 6.22 `dtp_ctm_rand_all_scenarios_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM all scenarios

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized routes across all CTM ports spanning both wire-OR and P2P: every selected output's `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` holds the source mask of the iteration
* Random source/destination draws across CLA and CTP classes: a wire-OR iteration draws one or two sources and two to four outputs, a P2P iteration one source and one output
* All observed routing matches the CTM reference model for every random configuration
* No routing corruption or state leakage between successive random configurations (every route is programmed over cleared selects)
* Wire-OR superposition: two sources selected on the same outputs and pulsed in the same cycle fire exactly the modelled outputs

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Draw the mode, one or two sources (wire-OR) or one (P2P), and the outputs; the sources stay out of the output set.
b. Clear every `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+`, write the source mask into each selected output's register via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`, and read each back to confirm it.
c. Pulse the drawn sources in the same cycle via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs (a chiplet pulls each CTP source's shared wire) inside an activity window over every output.
d. Verify the outputs that start requesting after the sources are pulsed are exactly the reference model's prediction, each asserting once, and that no unselected output fired.
e. Verify every selected output is idle again at the end of the window.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_csr_chk` |Every selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` reads back the iteration's source mask
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_route_chk` |All observed routing matches the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_or_chk` |Two sources selected on the same outputs and pulsed in the same cycle fire exactly the modelled outputs (one merged pulse each) with no unselected output
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_noleak_chk` |No unselected output fires and every selected output is idle at the end of each iteration's window
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random mixed-mode configuration routes exactly as modeled with correct wire-OR superposition and no inter-configuration leakage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-623-dtp_ctm_rand_wire_or_only_test]]
===== 6.23 `dtp_ctm_rand_wire_or_only_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM wire-OR only

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized wire-OR (multicast) routing only: every iteration draws one or two sources and two to four outputs, and each selected output's `+CT_SRC[dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` holds the source mask (a multi-bit register is a multi-source OR onto that output)
* Random source pulses fan out to all selected destinations simultaneously
* Wire-OR superposition: two sources pulsed in the same cycle fire exactly the outputs that select them
* Non-selected destinations remain quiet for every random configuration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Draw one or two sources and two to four outputs (sources outside the output set).
b. Clear every `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+`, write the source mask into each selected output's register via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`, and read each back.
c. Pulse the drawn sources in the same cycle via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs (a chiplet pulls each CTP source's shared wire) inside an activity window over every output.
d. Verify the outputs that start requesting after the sources are pulsed are exactly the reference model's prediction, each asserting once, and that every selected destination asserted.
e. Verify non-selected destinations stayed idle and the selected ones are idle again at the end of the window.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_csr_chk` |Every selected output's `CT_DST_SELECT` reads back the iteration's source mask
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_route_chk` |All selected destinations assert per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_or_chk` |Two sources selected on the same outputs and pulsed in the same cycle fire exactly the modelled outputs
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_nospur_chk` |No non-selected destination toggles
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_receive_chk` |Every wire-OR source's `ct_dst` rises three clocks after its wire or CLA request asserts and never at the release (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random wire-OR configuration multicasts correctly with correct superposition and no spurious destination toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-624-dtp_ctm_rand_p2p_only_test]]
===== 6.24 `dtp_ctm_rand_p2p_only_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM P2P only

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized single-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks restricted to point-to-point routing only
* Random request/acknowledge handshakes complete at exactly the selected destination
* No cross-route leakage between independent point-to-point routes: each iteration programs a second, disjoint route alongside the drawn one and pulses each alone
* Coverage of diverse single-bit destination positions across sources

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Generate random `CT_DST_SELECT` values constrained to exactly one set bit per active source (P2P-only).
b. Write all active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back to confirm a single destination bit is set.
c. Drive randomized handshakes on random source ports via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs.
d. Verify each request/ack completes only at its configured single destination.
e. Program a second point-to-point route on a disjoint source and destination without clearing the first, pulse each source alone, and verify the other route's destination stays quiet (no cross-route leakage).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_csr_chk` |Every randomized P2P mask reads back with exactly one destination bit set
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_route_chk` |Each request/ack completes at the selected destination per the reference model
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_noleak_chk` |No cross-route leakage between independent point-to-point routes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random point-to-point configuration completes its handshake at exactly the configured destination with no cross-route leakage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-625-dtp_ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.25 `dtp_ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM CLA to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTM routes restricted to internal CLA sources targeting external CTP destinations
* Random single-bit and multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks and random trigger timing within the CLA-to-CTP class: each iteration draws a pulse width of 2 to 8 system clocks and an idle lead of 0 to 3
* Selected CTP destinations match the random configuration; non-selected stay quiet
* Every CLA-source x CTP-destination route driven alone within one test: the iterations walk a seeded order of the routes, starting each from the first route not yet driven alone; only a window whose single source is the route's own retires it, and the last pass continues until every route is retired (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` counts them)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Randomize `CT_DST_SELECT` masks for CLA sources, with destination bits restricted to CTP ports.
b. Write the active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive CLA triggers via the OCAH-local CTM BFM with randomized timing.
d. Observe CTP destinations via the OCAH-local CTP monitor and verify the selected CTP ports respond.
e. Sample the CLA source x CTP destination cross of `cg_ctm_route` for every route the matrix carried from a single source.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_csr_chk` |Every randomized CLA-to-CTP mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_route_chk` |Selected CTP destinations respond per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CTP destination toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random CLA-to-CTP configuration routes correctly to its selected CTP destinations with no spurious activity, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors; every seed drives every CLA-source x CTP-destination route alone, which closes the CLA source x CTP destination cross of `cg_ctm_route` on the commercial simulators.

'''''

[[sec-626-dtp_ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.26 `dtp_ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM CTP to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTM routes restricted to external CTP sources targeting internal CLA destinations
* Random single-bit and multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks and random trigger timing within the CTP-to-CLA class: each iteration draws a pulse width of 2 to 8 system clocks and an idle lead of 0 to 3; a point-to-point CTP source holds CT_Req_in for that width and until CT_Ack_out has been asserted for a seeded 4 to 16 cycles
* Selected CLA destinations match the random configuration; non-selected stay quiet
* Every CTP-source x CLA-destination route driven alone within one test: the iterations walk a seeded order of the routes, starting each from the first route not yet driven alone; only a window whose single source is the route's own retires it, and the last pass continues until every route is retired (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` counts them)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Randomize `CT_DST_SELECT` masks for CTP sources, with destination bits restricted to CLA channels.
b. Write the active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive CTP triggers via the OCAH-local CTP BFM with randomized timing.
d. Observe CLA destinations via the OCAH-local CTM monitor and verify the selected CLA channels respond.
e. Sample the CTP source x CLA destination cross of `cg_ctm_route` for every route the matrix carried from a single source.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_csr_chk` |Every randomized CTP-to-CLA mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_route_chk` |Selected CLA destinations respond per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CLA destination toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random CTP-to-CLA configuration routes correctly to its selected CLA destinations with no spurious activity, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors; every seed drives every CTP-source x CLA-destination route alone, which closes the CTP source x CLA destination cross of `cg_ctm_route` on the commercial simulators.

'''''

[[sec-627-dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test]]
===== 6.27 `dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM CSR deterministic sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of CTM generated-regblock byte-enable and masked-write condition holes that random selection can miss
* Full-word, per-byte, and masked writes to `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` with readback after each
* Reserved bits read back as zero and are unaffected by writes
* The hole word of every `+CT_SRC[N]+` slot, which reads 0 and ignores writes
* A seeded word of the matrix aperture past the `+CT_SRC[N]+` registers and one past the last CTP window, whose write and read each complete with DECERR
* Internal route decode tracks each programmed `CT_DST_SELECT` value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle and disable all CTM source routing.
. Sweep every `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` deterministically via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Apply the full-word patterns (single bits, the full select mask, a fixed and a seeded multi-bit mask, the all-ones word, and the inverted patterns, which drive reserved bits [31:26] to 1), then every byte strobe (0x1, 0x2, 0x4, 0x8) with a seeded word over a seeded base.
b. Read back after each write and compare the full word: the defined field holds the expected value and the reserved bits read 0.
c. Write all ones to the hole word past the register in its slot and read 0 back; the register keeps its last strobed word.
. Write all ones to a seeded word in the matrix aperture past its registers and to a seeded word past the last CTP window, and read each back: every response is DECERR.
. For two seeded output registers holding the seeded multi-bit select, pulse an input the select names and see the output fire per the reference model, then pulse an input it does not name and see the output stay quiet.
. Disable all CTM source routing before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_csr_chk` |Full-word and byte-strobe readbacks, compared as the full word, match the expected `CT_DST_SELECT` values
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_rsvd_chk` |Reserved bits [31:26] read 0 after the all-ones, inverted, and strobed writes
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_route_chk` |Two swept output registers holding a multi-bit select fire for an input the select names and stay quiet for one it does not, against the reference model
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_hole_chk` |The hole word of every slot completes the write and the read with OKAY and reads 0, and the write leaves the slot's register on its last strobed word
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_unmapped_chk` |The write and the read of each unmapped word complete with DECERR (`CHK-XTRIG-CSR`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every byte-enable and masked-write condition in the CTM regblock is exercised and reads back correctly with reserved bits clear, every hole reading 0, every unmapped word answering DECERR, and route decode matching, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-628-dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test]]
===== 6.28 `dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM all-source select coverage

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of per-source select mapping for every defined CTM source-select CSR (`CT_SRC[0].CONFIG_0` through `CT_SRC[25].CONFIG_0`)
* Single-destination, paired-destination, and inverted-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` masks written and read back at every source index
* No source index aliases another CTM source register
* Direct exercise of all generated CTM source-select mapping code in the bench configuration
* One routed pulse per source after its select sweep, judged against the CTM model

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert power-on reset, bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle, and disable all CTM source routing.
. For every defined CTM source index, via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write a single-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` mask, read back, and compare the defined destination-select field.
b. Write a paired-destination mask, read back, and compare.
c. Write an inverted-destination mask, read back, and compare.
d. Confirm the written value affects only this source register and no neighboring `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` (no aliasing).
e. Program a single-destination route from the source, pulse it, and judge the output window against the CTM model.
. Sample per-source select coverage so every `CT_SRC1..25_CONFIG_0` mapping is touched.
. Disable all CTM source routing again before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_csr_chk` |Every defined source-select CSR accepts and returns the single/paired/inverted mask
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_alias_chk` |No source index aliases another CTM source register
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_route_chk` |After each source's select sweep, a single-destination route programmed from that source delivers one pulse to its destination and to no other output (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every defined CTM source-select CSR is touched with single, paired, and inverted masks and reads back correctly with no aliasing across source indices, and every source routes one pulse to its selected destination, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-629-dtp_xtrig_wire_or_bus_test]]
===== 6.29 `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_bus_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTPs on one shared wire-OR wire

*Test Coverage*:

* Two to four CTPs sharing one open-drain wire at one INVERT sense, the passes alternating the sense, each receiving every assertion of the wire once, three clocks after the wire moves to its asserted level
* A member's own stretched pull heard by every member, the transmitter included
* A chiplet's pull heard by every member
* A chiplet's pull and the transmitter's pull overlapping into one continuous assertion, received once by every member

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw two to four CTPs, one STRETCH_MULT (0..7), one internal source, and one internal destination per member; the INVERT sense is 0 on even passes and 1 on odd ones. Program every member wire-OR at that sense and stretch via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`, rest the shared wire at the pull of the board built for that sense, route the internal source to the first member (the transmitter), and route each member's receive to its internal destination.
. Pulse the internal source. Verify the transmitter's output enable window, every member's `ct_dst` exactly three clocks after the window opens, and one pulse on every internal destination; nothing else moves.
. Let the chiplet on the wire pull it for one to five clocks. Verify every member's `ct_dst` exactly three clocks after the pull, once, and one pulse on every internal destination.
. Let the chiplet pull the wire, pulse the internal source while it holds, and release the chiplet after the transmitter's window has closed. Verify every member's `ct_dst` rises once, three clocks after the chiplet's pull, and every internal destination pulses once.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_wire_or_bus_csr_chk` |Every member's CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT and every route register read back their programmed values
|`xtrig_wire_or_bus_route_chk` |The outputs that fire in each window are exactly the reference model's prediction: the transmitter's enable and every member's internal destination (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION`)
|`xtrig_wire_or_bus_receive_chk` |Every member's `ct_dst` rises once per continuous assertion of the wire, three clocks after the wire moves, whether the transmitter, the chiplet, or both pulled it (`CHK-XTRIG-WIRE`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every member of the shared wire receives each assertion exactly once at the receive latency, overlapping pulls merge into one assertion, and the routed destinations match the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-630-dtp_xtrig_axi_outstanding_test]]
===== 6.30 `dtp_xtrig_axi_outstanding_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Outstanding CSR accesses

*Test Coverage*:

* Seven reads, and seven writes, outstanding at once through the shared AXI-Lite master's pipelined operation with RREADY or BREADY held: the port's spill registers (<<sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test>>) and the crossbar demux behind them fill until the port drops ARREADY, AWREADY and WREADY, and the third access of each burst reaches each crossbar subordinate in turn, the matrix, every CTP and the decode-error subordinate, while the responses ahead of it wait
* A write, a read and a second write to one register block a cycle apart, on a CTP and on the matrix, swept across their launch offsets, so the block holds a read and a write together and the crossbar demux holds an AW the block refuses (the demux AW lock)
* Two reads held behind RREADY, a third read waiting at a register block, and a write to the same block landing while that read's response waits, so the block holds two accesses
* Every AxPROT bit set and cleared on both address channels; a read and a write to an unmapped word with every address bit above the CSR map set, each behind a mapped access, answered DECERR; a write at an unaligned address with the strobe from that byte up waiting in the AW spill register; a read of every register-holding subordinate at an unaligned byte address, answered OKAY with the word that contains it
* Every CT_SRC select holding a drawn mask while the bursts run, so the held read data carries the upper select bits; no trigger is pulsed, and the selects read 0 again at the end
* A write to the matrix and one to an unmapped word with W three cycles behind AW, and two matrix reads one cycle apart

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Idle all manual AXI-Lite master outputs after reset. Every access below goes through the master's pipelined operation, which launches each beat at its seeded delay or as soon as the beat ahead of it on its channel is accepted, and holds BREADY and RREADY for the given cycles after the first response. For every pipeline, verify each access answers DECERR exactly when no subordinate decodes its word and OKAY otherwise, every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` does not advance), and the port counters `xtrig_axil_aw_stall_count`, `xtrig_axil_w_stall_count` and `xtrig_axil_ar_stall_count` advance by the stall cycles the VIP saw on each channel.
. Write a seeded mask to every CT_SRC select in one pipeline.
. For each of the eighteen crossbar subordinates in a seeded order, issue seven reads of seeded words at seeded AxPROT values with the third to that subordinate, holding RREADY for 12..16 cycles after the first RVALID. Verify the port held ARREADY low (`xtrig_axil_ar_stall_count` advanced).
. For each subordinate in a fresh seeded order, issue seven writes of seeded words the same way, the fifth at a seeded unaligned byte with the strobe from that byte up, holding BREADY for 12..16 cycles. Verify the port held AWREADY and WREADY low.
. On a seeded CTP, issue a STRETCH_MULT write, a CONFIG read, and a second STRETCH_MULT write, with the read launched 0..2 cycles and the second write's AW 0..2 cycles after the first write and its W 0 or 2 cycles behind that AW; then, at the same offsets, a CONFIG read, a STRETCH_MULT write and a second CONFIG read. Repeat on the matrix with a write to one seeded CT_SRC select and reads of another. Verify the crossbar demux held a refused AW at least once across the sweep (`xtrig_demux_aw_lock`).
. On the same CTP and on the matrix, issue reads of two other subordinates, a read of the block and a write to it, with the write launched 6, 8, 10, 12 or 14 cycles after the start, holding RREADY for 12..16 cycles.
. Issue a CONFIG read at AxPROT 0, a read of `0xFFFF_FC00` at AxPROT 7, a STRETCH_MULT write at AxPROT 0, a write to `0xFFFF_FC00` at AxPROT 7, a CONFIG read and a STRETCH_MULT write; then one read of each register-holding subordinate at a seeded unaligned byte of a seeded word.
. Issue a CT_SRC write and an unmapped write, each with W three cycles behind AW, and two CT_SRC reads with the second AR one cycle after the first.
. Write 0 to every CT_SRC select in one pipeline.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_outstanding_resp_chk` |Every access answers DECERR exactly when no subordinate decodes its word and OKAY otherwise, and mapped reads match the `xtrig_csr` scoreboard feature (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_port_chk` |Every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds, and the port stall counters equal the VIP's stall cycles on each channel (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_stall_chk` |Each read burst drops ARREADY at the port (`CHK-XTRIG-AR-STALL`), and each write burst AWREADY and WREADY (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_aw_lock_chk` |The crossbar demux holds a refused AW at least once in the sweep (`CHK-XTRIG-AW-LOCK`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every outstanding access completes with the response its address decodes to, the port stalls when the bursts back up, and the demux AW lock engages, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[dtp-formal-plan]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

Formal property checking corroborates the control-logic rows of the
traceability matrix; it does not replace the simulation credit. The open-path
flow runs SymbiYosys through the runner's formal mode
(`tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification) and follows the property
style in `hw/common/dv/docs/formal-property-style.adoc`. `dtp_formal_cfg.toml`
and `testlists/formal.toml` are the launch entry; the properties, the bind
files, the task files and the reset environments live under `formal/`. The
plan names nine targets, T1 to T9, over three formal tops: `dtp` for the
JTAG-domain blocks, `jtag2axi` for the bridge control, and
`cross_trigger_network` for the single-clock cross-trigger logic. Every
target row names the traceability rows it corroborates, the app checks that
apply, the path that runs them, and the item `run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode
formal` launches. The `dtp` blocks share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, whose task
tags select one block's property module; `dtp_jtag2axi.sby` and
`dtp_cross_trigger_network.sby` beside it carry the other two tops, and each top
has its own environment module.

[[dtp-formal-targets]]
==== Targets

A target is a described block whose behaviour is a boolean over a small
control state: a state machine, a handshake, a gating term, a register with a
fixed access policy. Data movement is out (<<dtp-formal-exclusions>>).

[cols="1,3,3,2,2",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Described block |Rows |Formal top and item |Path
|T1 TAP controller |`jtag_tap_ctrlr`: the sixteen-state Moore machine and the scan-control flags |F1.1, F1.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_tap_fpv` |Open
|T2 Instruction register and TDR selection |`jtag_inst_reg` (6-bit shift, 64-bit one-hot decode, IDCODE on reset) and the PTAP select and TDO multiplexer terms: bypass for undefined opcodes and for disabled features, one TDR selected per instruction, zero-length bypass, the read-only IDCODE and CAPS captures |F1.3, F1.4, F1.5 (selection), F2.5 |`dtp`, `dtp_ir_fpv` |Open
|T3 Reset hierarchy and persistence |`jtag_trst_n = trst_n AND pwr_on_rst_ni`, `rst_n = !test_logic_reset`, `chrst_n` under `jtag_tmp`; `jtag_tmp_status_reg`; `jtag_ic_reset_reg` with `reset_hold`; `jtag_3dcr_reg` with `config_hold` |F1.2, F1.6, F2.1, F2.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_reset_fpv` |Open
|T4 STAP selection and debug disable |The PTAP 3DCR select, the four `jtag_stap` instances (I/O, SMC, SEP, extra) with their 3DCR and lockup flops, the `stap_host` return path in `jtag_intf_unit`, the two-flop `dbg_disable_i` synchronizers |F3.3, F3.4, F3.5, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_stap_fpv` |Open; STAP chain order and disable wiring also as licensed connectivity
|T5 iJTAG SIB network and debug disable |The three `prim_jtag_sib_mux_post` instances (DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD) |F3.1, F3.2, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_sib_fpv` |Open
|T6 Debug control and clock stop |`jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` with its `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer; `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |F2.3, F2.4, F5.7 |`dtp`, `dtp_dbgctl_fpv`; `cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_clkstop_fpv` |Open
|T7 JTAG2AXI bridge control |`jtag2axi`: the seven-state request machine, the TCK-side admit counters, the ACLK outstanding counters, the status encoder, the disable gate and its flush |F4.3 (status encoding), F4.4 (control), F4.5 |`jtag2axi`, `jtag2axi_ctrl_fpv` |Open for the control with the AXI side free; the CDC transport and the mid-transaction reset as licensed X-propagation and reset checks
|T8 Cross-trigger port and matrix |`ctp_handshake_ctrl` (sender and receiver), `ctp_core` mode multiplexers and wire-OR edge detect, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, one `ctm_src_selector` slice |F5.1, F5.2, F5.3, F5.6 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_xtrig_fpv` |Open
|T9 Cross-trigger CSR access |The CTN address map into the AXI-Lite crossbar and its error subordinate; the generated register blocks' field access, strobes and reset values |F5.4, F5.5 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_csr_fpv` |Open for the address-map contract and the field policy of one CTP and one CTM slice; the full register sweep derived from the SystemRDL as the licensed register app
|===

[[dtp-formal-exclusions]]
==== Exclusions

[cols="3,4",options="header",]
|===
|Logic |Reason and where the credit lives
|JTAG2AXI data path: the 132-bit shared shift register with a variable active length, the byte-lane shifts by `addr mod BeatBytes`, the 56-bit series address increment and its wrap, the `DEADBEEF` pending-read capture |Wide arithmetic and per-bit loops; a bounded proof over them is a data check the F4.1 and F4.2 scenarios make with data verification. The bridge target proves control only.
|The five gray-coded clearable CDC FIFOs with three-stage synchronizers and the coupled reset controller inside `axi_cdc_clearable` |Third-party IP with its own correctness argument; the open path takes the AXI side as free inputs. A reset that lands mid-transaction leaves the request machine waiting with no recovery other than Test-Logic-Reset; that path is a licensed reset and X-propagation check, not an open-path property.
|Boundary-scan data and the external iJTAG instrument chains |Loopbacks in `tb_top`; the design carries selection and gating only, which T2 and T5 cover.
|`ctp_pulse_stretcher` at large `STRETCH_MULT` |A 16-bit down counter, up to 65 536 cycles. End-to-end proofs assume `STRETCH_MULT <= 4`; the counter's reload and terminal behaviour is one inductive property of its own.
|The full 26 x 26 cross-trigger matrix |A generate of identical selector slices; one slice with a free select mask stands for the array.
|The AXI-Lite crossbar internals (demux address-write lock, per-port FIFOs) |Vendor IP; T9 checks the address-map contract at the CTN port and the register blocks behind it.
|The pad-ring wire-OR bus |The shared wire and its pull live outside the RTL; the receive polarity is the port's own and T8 states it on the synchronized pad input. A property over several chiplets on one wire needs an environment model of the bus and is not a DUT property.
|TDO retiming and the lockup flops |Negedge flops are ordinary state under `multiclock on`; the timing margin they buy is not a property.
|Deriving `dbg_disable_t`, the adopter-defined layout of the IC_RESET external slice, real downstream TAPs |Integration level, as for the simulation bench.
|===

[[dtp-formal-properties]]
==== Properties by target

Every label follows the style chapter: `ast_` assertions, `asm_` assumptions,
`cov_` covers, each a boolean over current and `$past` values. Priority P0
marks the properties that need an unbounded proof (`prove`) before any
release; P1 and P2 sign off on a bounded result at the target's depth
(<<dtp-formal-depths>>). The P0 set is the security-gating set plus the reset
hierarchy: every disable field gates exactly its path, and no reset source
reaches a register the standard reserves for another.

T1 is the property module in `formal/props/dtp_tap_props.sv`, bound to
`jtag_tap_ctrlr` by `formal/props/dtp_tap_bind.sv`:

[cols="3,3,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Label |Checks |Row |Prio
|`ast_tap_state_one_hot` |The state register holds exactly one state |F1.1 |P0
|`ast_tap_five_tms_high_reach_tlr` |Five consecutive TMS-high cycles reach Test-Logic-Reset from any state |F1.2 |P0
|`ast_tap_tlr_holds_on_tms_high`, `ast_tap_tlr_exits_to_rti`, `ast_tap_rti_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_shift_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_update_dr_only_from_exit`, `ast_tap_update_ir_only_from_exit` |The legal TMS arcs into and out of the reset, idle, shift and update states |F1.1 |P1
|`ast_tap_select_exclusive`, `ast_tap_moore_flags_follow_state`, `ast_tap_negedge_flags_follow_state` |The DR and IR selects are exclusive; the Moore flags and the negedge-clocked scan-control flags follow the state |F1.1 |P1
|===

The T1 item also binds the shared JTAG checker to `dtp` on the primary TAP
(`formal/props/dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_jtag_vip/sva/ocah_jtag_fv.sv`),
whose slave rules it asserts: `ast_jtag_state_onehot`,
`ast_jtag_state_next_legal` (every transition follows the IEEE 1149.1
controller diagram), `ast_jtag_trst_tlr`, `ast_jtag_tlr_tms5`,
`ast_jtag_tdo_oen_shift_only` and `ast_jtag_tdo_stable_while_tck_high` (the
enable, and TDO while the enable is active, change on the falling edge alone),
with the covers `cov_jtag_shift_dr`, `cov_jtag_shift_ir`,
`cov_jtag_tlr_from_five_tms_high` and `cov_jtag_tdo_driven`. The T7 and T9
items bind the shared AXI checker
(`hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_axi_vip/sva/ocah_axi_fv.sv`) with the design's side
asserted: on `jtag2axi` the request machine's master rules,
`ast_axi_<ch>_valid_reset_low`, `ast_axi_<ch>_valid_held`,
`ast_axi_<ch>_payload_stable` and `ast_axi_<ch>_within_capacity` for the AW,
W and AR channels plus the AW and AR burst-legality rules; on
`cross_trigger_network` the slave rules `ast_axi_<ch>_valid_reset_low`,
`ast_axi_<ch>_valid_held` and `ast_axi_<ch>_payload_stable` for the B and R
channels, `ast_axi_b_not_before_aw`, `ast_axi_r_not_before_ar`,
`ast_axi_axil_b_resp_legal` and `ast_axi_axil_r_resp_legal`, with the five
`cov_axi_<ch>_handshake` covers (<<dtp-formal-assumptions>>). Every report
names them under the bind instance, for example
`u_ocah_axi_fv.gen_axi_b_valid_held.ast_axi_b_valid_held`.

The remaining targets, one property module per block under
`formal/props/`, with the intents the labels carry. T2 and T3 bind to
`jtag_ptap`, T4 and T5 to `jtag_intf_unit` with the four STAPs and the three
SIBs packed into per-instance vectors, T6 to `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` and
`ctn_clock_stop_ctrl`, T7 to `jtag2axi`, and T8 and T9 to
`cross_trigger_network` with external port 0 and matrix slice 0 standing for
their arrays:

[cols="1,3,4,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Module |Property intents |Prio
|T2 |`dtp_ir_props.sv` on `jtag_inst_reg` and the PTAP select terms |`ast_ir_decoded_one_hot`: the decoded instruction is one-hot. `ast_ir_capture_loads_01`: Capture-IR loads `6'b000001`. `ast_ir_update_decodes_shift_reg`: after Update-IR the decoded instruction is `1 << shift_reg`. `ast_ir_reset_is_idcode`: Test-Logic-Reset, TRST and power-on reset leave IDCODE decoded. `ast_tdr_select_one_hot`: at most one register select is high in a DR scan, each select equals the DR select under its own instruction, the bypass register is also clocked by IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans and by ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS scans with the PTAP 3DCR select set, and the TDO multiplexer reads the register of the instruction. `ast_undefined_selects_bypass`: every opcode outside the defined set, and every defined opcode whose feature parameter is 0, selects the bypass register. `ast_zlb_bypasses_retimer`: under ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS in Shift-DR with the PTAP 3DCR select clear, TDO follows TDI in the same step; with the select set the bypass register is selected as under BYPASS. `ast_idcode_caps_read_only`: the IDCODE and CAPS captures load the parameter constants and no Update-DR changes them. Covers: each of the 64 opcodes decoded, the bypass selection for one undefined opcode, a zero-length shift, a read of each capability TDR. |P1; the decode one-hot and the undefined-to-bypass rule P0
|T3 |`dtp_reset_props.sv` on the PTAP reset terms, `jtag_tmp`, `jtag_tmp_status_reg`, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, `jtag_3dcr_reg` |`ast_trst_or_por_resets_tap`: either source low forces Test-Logic-Reset and the IDCODE instruction. `ast_tlr_asserts_rst_n`: `rst_n` is low exactly while the negedge-sampled state is Test-Logic-Reset. `ast_chrst_follows_rst_unless_persistent`: `chrst_n` equals `rst_n` while persistence is off and stays high in Test-Logic-Reset while it is on. `ast_tmp_on_only_by_clamp_hold`, `ast_tmp_off_by_release_or_escape`: the two TMP transitions and no other; the bypass escape needs Update-IR, an explicit BYPASS opcode and the escape bit. `ast_tmp_survives_tlr`: Test-Logic-Reset leaves the TMP state unchanged. `ast_ic_reset_hold_blocks_tlr`: with `reset_hold` 0, Test-Logic-Reset leaves `reset_enable` and `reset_control` unchanged; with `reset_hold` 1 it restores them to all-ones. `ast_ic_reset_trst_clears_all`: TRST restores every IC_RESET bit including `reset_hold`. `ast_ic_reset_ovrd_is_not_enable`: `.ovrd` is the inversion of `reset_enable` and `.val` equals `reset_control`. `ast_3dcr_config_hold_blocks_tlr`: the PTAP 3DCR survives Test-Logic-Reset while `config_hold` is set and clears on TRST. Covers: persistence on then off by each exit, an IC_RESET override held across a Test-Logic-Reset, a 3DCR held across a Test-Logic-Reset. |P0
|T4 |`dtp_stap_props.sv` on the PTAP 3DCR, each `jtag_stap`, and the `stap_host` terms of `jtag_intf_unit` |`ast_stap_disable_forces_sel_low`, `ast_stap_disable_tdo_low`, `ast_stap_disable_blocks_3dcr_update`: with the STAP's disable field high, `stap_sel` is 0, `host_tdo_o` is 0, and the 3DCR update register holds its value. `ast_stap_unselected_parks_tms`: with `stap_sel` 0, `host_tap_ctrl_o.tms` equals `tms_hold`; with 1 it follows the client TMS. `ast_stap_tdo_oen_only_when_selected_shifting`: `host_tdo_oen_o` is `stap_sel AND shift_en`. `ast_stap_host_disable_loops_back`: with `stap_host` disabled the PTAP scan input is the chain's own output and every host scan-control enable is 0. `ast_ptap_stap_select_routes_tdo`: with the PTAP 3DCR select set, TDO comes from the STAP chain, else from the TDR multiplexer. `ast_stap_chain_quiet_unselected`: with the PTAP 3DCR select clear, the select, capture, shift and update enables of the STAP chain's scan control are 0. `ast_dbg_disable_sync_fail_closed`: each synchronizer resets to 1 and follows its input after two `tck` edges. Covers: the I/O, SMC, SEP and extra STAP each selected; a STAP disabled while selected; the return path disabled during a shift, and enabled during a shift with the PTAP select set. |P0
|T5 |`dtp_sib_props.sv` on the three SIBs |`ast_sib_disable_forces_en_low`, `ast_sib_disable_blocks_update`, `ast_sib_disable_host_quiet`: with the SIB's field high, the segment enable is 0, the SIB bit does not update, and the host `capture_en`, `shift_en`, `update_en`, `select` and `host_scan_out_o` are 0. `ast_sib_open_routes_host`: with the enable set, the client scan output is the host scan input, else the SIB's own bit. `ast_sib_host_ctrl_follows_client`: the host enables equal the client enables AND `select` while enabled. `ast_sib_chain_order`: the DFD SIB's input is the non-secure SIB's output, whose input is the secure SIB's output. Covers: every SIB pattern (eight), each SIB disabled while open. |P0
|T6 |`dtp_dbgctl_props.sv` on `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg`; `dtp_ctn_clkstop_props.sv` on `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |`ast_dbgctl_rw_bits_hold`: bits 3:0 change only on an Update-DR of the DEBUG_CONTROL TDR. `ast_dbgctl_ro_bit_captures_status`: bit 4 captures the synchronized `cla_clock_stop` and ignores the shifted value. `ast_dbgctl_outputs_follow_update_reg`: the four outputs equal the update register bits. `ast_clkstop_or_after_three`: `stop_clks_o` equals the OR of `jtag_clock_stop_i` and every `clk_stop_req_i` bit sampled three `clk_i` cycles earlier. `ast_cla_status_excludes_jtag`: `cla_clock_stop_o` is the OR of the request bits alone, with no register. `ast_clkstop_low_in_reset`: `stop_clks_o` is 0 while `rst_ni` is low. Covers: a JTAG stop with no CLA request, a CLA request with no JTAG stop, the boot-stall override with each stall value. |P1
|T7 |`dtp_jtag2axi_ctrl_props.sv` on `jtag2axi`, one bind serving the three instances |`ast_j2a_state_valid`: the request machine holds one of its seven states. `ast_j2a_valid_only_in_send_states`: the TCK-side `aw_valid`, `w_valid` and `ar_valid` requests are high only in the address and data states. `ast_j2a_idle_after_status`: UPDATE_STATUS returns to IDLE in one cycle. `ast_j2a_resp_only_while_waiting`: a response beat from a responder that follows the shared checker's rules arrives no earlier than the request's last handshake and no later than the state that consumes it, so it depends on the assumed responder rules and fails without them. `ast_j2a_status_encodes_resp`: OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR responses map to SUCCESS, SLVERR and DECERR, any other code to SLVERR. `ast_j2a_series_status_sticky`: the first series error status holds across later series completions until the series-control reset bit, a SINGLE_OP completion leaves the series status unchanged, the full flag holds until a reset write, and a reset write with no completion beside it returns the status to SUCCESS and clears the full flag. `ast_j2a_series_error_holds`: an error code holds until the series-control reset bit. `ast_j2a_one_read_in_flight`: at most one series read is in flight, and only while the machine waits for its data. `ast_j2a_admit_bounded_by_depth`: the in-flight and pushed counters never exceed the pipeline depth, itself clamped to `FIFO_DEPTH`. `ast_j2a_series_ops_are_axi`: the series mode never holds the reserved op code, and every request-FIFO push carries READ or WRITE. `ast_j2a_outstanding_saturates`: at their saturation value the ACLK outstanding counters hold the CDC ready low, so they never wrap. `ast_j2a_disable_holds_idle`: after a cycle with the disable high and the machine idle, the machine is idle, the single-request buffer, both series FIFOs and the read counters are empty, and no Update-DR latches while the disable is high. `ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`: a disable that arrives outside IDLE does not abort the transaction in flight: the send states keep their valids, a response completes into UPDATE_STATUS, and IDLE is entered from UPDATE_STATUS alone. `ast_j2a_release_replays_nothing`: an idle machine with an empty single buffer and request FIFO stays idle, so a released disable issues nothing until a new Update-DR. Covers: a single write and a single read to completion, a series of `FIFO_DEPTH` reads admitted, a SLVERR and a DECERR status, a clean completion while an error code is held, an error status held across a later OKAY series completion, a series-control update with the reserved op code, a disable landing in each non-idle state. |P0 for the disable and admit properties, P1 otherwise
|T8 |`dtp_ctn_xtrig_props.sv` on `ctp_handshake_ctrl`, `ctp_core`, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, `ctm_src_selector` |`ast_hs_sender_states`, `ast_hs_receiver_states`: the two machines hold legal states and take only the four-phase arcs. `ast_hs_req_stable_until_ack`: `ct_req_out` holds until the synchronized ack. `ast_hs_ack_mirrors_req`: the receiver asserts ack on a synchronized request and drops it when the request drops. `ast_hs_dst_is_one_pulse`: `ct_dst` rises only from the idle receiver on a synchronized request and lasts one cycle. `ast_hs_busy_lags_state`: `busy` is the registered not-idle of either machine. `ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`: `reset_i` returns the sender to IDLE with `ct_req_out` low, and leaves the receiver untouched. `ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`: the wire-OR and point-to-point pad enables are mutually exclusive and match `CONFIG.MODE`. `ast_ctp_wire_or_sense`: the optional inversion sits after the synchronizer, so the sensed wire rests at 1 in either sense. `ast_ctp_wire_or_edge_pulse`: in wire-OR mode `ct_dst` is the sensed wire having moved from rest to asserted one cycle earlier, the falling edge of the raw pad for INVERT=0 and its rising edge for INVERT=1, and the release edge yields nothing; in point-to-point mode it is the handshake's `ct_dst`. `ast_stretch_width`: a trigger holds the output for `STRETCH_MULT + 1` cycles and a new trigger reloads the counter. `ast_ctm_slice_route`: `ct_src_o` equals the registered OR of `ct_dst AND select`, and an all-zero select isolates the output. `ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`: `STATUS.REQ_IN` and `ACK_IN` equal the synchronized de-inverted pad values, `REQ_OUT` and `ACK_OUT` the de-inverted registered outputs; in wire-OR mode `REQ_OUT` and `ACK_OUT` read 0. Covers: a full four-phase cycle against the peer model, a stretched wire-OR pulse of the maximum assumed width, a wire-OR receive in each sense, a route from an internal source to an external port and back, reset in each mode. |P1
|T9 |`dtp_ctn_csr_props.sv` on `cross_trigger_network` and one CTP and one CTM register block |`ast_csr_unmapped_decerr`: an address outside the matrix register extent (`0x000` to `0x0CF`) and the port windows (`0x200` to `0x2FF`) completes with DECERR on both channels, including `0x0D0` to `0x1FF` of the matrix aperture; every byte address inside them completes with OKAY. `ast_csr_ctp_window`: an address in `0x200 + 16n` to `0x20F + 16n` reaches CTP `n` alone, and a crossbar master port carries a request only while the subordinate port holds it accepted and unanswered, from the cycle after its address handshake, and never again once a master port has released it. `ast_csr_ctm_readback`: the matrix block dispatches the word address it receives, its readback follows address bits 7:2, and the response carries the dispatched readback. `ast_csr_reset_values`: CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT and every CT_DST_SELECT read 0 after reset. `ast_csr_status_write_ignored`: a write to STATUS returns OKAY and changes nothing. `ast_csr_strobe_masks_bytes`: a write changes only the strobed bytes. `ast_csr_read_after_write`: a read after a completed write returns the written field value. Covers: a DECERR write and read, a DECERR read past the matrix register extent, a CTP and a CTM read-after-write, a masked write, a write and a read that a master port has released before the subordinate port answers them. |P1
|===

[[dtp-formal-observations]]
==== Observations the properties settle

Four properties above pin RTL behaviour beyond what the design chapters
state.

* A disabled bridge gates only the IDLE arm of its AXI machine and flushes
  the buffers once IDLE is reached, so a transaction already in flight
  completes (`ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`).
* `stop_clks_o` is the OR of the requests through a two-flop synchronizer
  and an output flop, three `clk_i` cycles (`ast_clkstop_or_after_three`).
* `INT_CT_MODE[i] = 0` is the wire-OR pulse mode,
  `ModeWireOr = ~INT_CT_MODE[i]` (`ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`, checked
  per internal port).
* `CONFIG.RESET` returns only the sender machine to IDLE; the receiver has no
  reset arc (`ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`).

The proofs and cover traces add three points the chapters do not state.

* The bypass register's select term shares its default arm with the
  undefined opcodes, so IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans clock the
  bypass register too while TDO reads their own register
  (`ast_tdr_select_one_hot`).
* The TMP status register resets its bypass-escape bit (bit 0) to 0, as the
  example TMP control register of IEEE 1149.1-2013 does, so the escape exit
  is disabled after reset and needs a TMP_STATUS write first; an escape
  enabled by default would let a BYPASS load aimed at another device in the
  chain release CLAMP_HOLD (`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`).
* The instruction decode is `jtag_instruction_decoded_e'(2 ** shift)`. The
  open-path frontend sizes the operand of a type cast on its own, so the
  power is 32 bits wide in the model and an opcode above `0x1E` decodes to
  zero or to a sign-extended pattern, while Verilator sizes the operand to
  the cast type and decodes every opcode. The T2 module therefore assumes an
  opcode at or below `0x1E` at every Update-IR (`asm_ir_open_path_decode_range`)
  and covers that half of the instruction space; the zero-length bypass, the
  upper capability registers and the SEP and SMC bridge instructions have no
  open-path cover. The instruction package's own enum values use the shift
  form `DecodedIrWidth'(1'b1) << opcode`, which every frontend sizes alike
  (`formal-property-style.adoc`, Accepted constructs).

Two reset paths are checks for the licensed X-propagation app
(<<dtp-formal-apps>>): `cla_clock_stop_o` is combinational with no reset, and
the `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer inside `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` has its reset
tied high.

[[dtp-formal-assumptions]]
==== Assumptions and environments

Every top has one environment module bound at the top, in the shape of
`formal/fpv/sby/dtp_sby_env.sv`: every asynchronous reset is asserted up to
the first sampling edge, so each clock domain starts from its reset state,
and released there for the rest of the trace
(`asm_env_resets_asserted`, `asm_env_resets_released`). Each property module
adds `asm_initial_reset` from the macro layer on its own reset. Beyond that:

* `dtp`: `asm_env_scan_reset_inactive` holds the scan reset high.
  `dbg_disable_i`, `tms`, `tdi` and every host scan input are free, except
  that the return data of the external scan chains and of the downstream
  TAPs holds through the tck-high phase
  (`asm_env_scan_returns_hold_while_tck_high`), the shape of a JTAG slave's
  TDO. The AXI responder ports are free too: the JTAG-domain targets never
  reach them. The T1 item binds the shared JTAG checker `ocah_jtag_fv` on
  the primary TAP (`dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`), which assumes the host's phase
  rule, `asm_jtag_tms_tdi_stable_while_tck_high`, and asserts the TAP's rules
  (<<dtp-formal-properties>>); that item also reads `dtp_tap_sby_env.sv`,
  whose `asm_env_ir_open_path_decode_range` bounds the opcode an Update-IR
  loads to `0x1E`, as the T2 module does for the frontend sizing recorded in
  <<dtp-formal-observations>>: a wider opcode sets several instruction bits
  in the model, among them the zero-length bypass that routes TDO around its
  retimer. The T2 module adds
  `asm_ir_open_path_decode_range`, an opcode at or below `0x1E` at every
  Update-IR, for the frontend sizing recorded in <<dtp-formal-observations>>.
* `jtag2axi`, elaborated in the shape of the OTP AXI-Lite bridges (32-bit
  address and data, pipeline depth 3): the JTAG-side controls are free inputs
  constrained to the TAP's shape, `asm_j2a_scan_ctrl_exclusive` (capture,
  shift and update never overlap), `asm_j2a_update_follows_shift_or_capture`
  (an update is one cycle and closes a scan a capture opened),
  `asm_j2a_select_one_hot` (at most one bridge select), and
  `asm_j2a_controls_tck_synchronous` (the disable and the update enable hold
  the value the following posedge samples across the negedge, the shape of
  the DTP's synchronizer and TAP flags). Two cutpoints in the task file keep
  the target on the control. The shared shift register is free at every step,
  so an Update-DR presents any request without the seventy shift cycles that
  loading it through TDI costs. In the `bmc` and `cover` tasks the CDC's
  response side toward the request machine (`src_resp`) is free too, so a
  response needs no round trip through the clearable CDC, whose clear
  handshake after reset holds every channel for more than sixty steps before
  the first request can cross; the `prove` task keeps the CDC in the loop for
  the property-directed engine. The shared AXI checker `ocah_axi_fv`, bound
  by `dtp_jtag2axi_axi_fv_bind.sv` on the CDC's response side toward the
  request machine, constrains that responder in every task with its slave
  rules assumed: a B or R beat arrives only with a request outstanding
  (`asm_axi_b_not_before_aw`, `asm_axi_r_not_before_ar`), a valid holds
  with its payload until its ready (`asm_axi_b_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_r_valid_held`, `asm_axi_b_payload_stable`,
  `asm_axi_r_payload_stable`) and a valid is low in reset; the real CDC
  satisfies them, and `ast_j2a_resp_only_while_waiting` is the T7 property
  that holds only under them. The same instance asserts the request
  machine's master rules (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The AXI ports lie
  behind the CDC, whose clear handshake after reset keeps them idle within
  the bounded depth, so they carry no checker instance. The
  environment module adds what the protocol leaves open:
  `asm_j2a_src_r_single_beat` and `asm_j2a_r_single_beat`, since every
  request is a single beat. The request's size field is free, and on the
  32-bit top it admits a transfer wider than the bus; the bridge limits it to
  the bus width, so the checker's `ast_axi_aw_size_legal` and
  `ast_axi_ar_size_legal` hold with no assumption on the host. Every proof
  over the control logic holds for the real signals because the free ones
  include every value they can take.
* `cross_trigger_network`: the AXI-Lite manager is the DTP's bridge, so
  `asm_axil_one_outstanding` allows one write and one read in flight, and in
  the T9 item the shared AXI checker bound by `dtp_ctn_axi_fv_bind.sv` with
  its master rules assumed holds each valid with its payload until its ready
  (`asm_axi_aw_valid_held`, `asm_axi_w_valid_held`, `asm_axi_ar_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_aw_payload_stable`, `asm_axi_w_payload_stable`,
  `asm_axi_ar_payload_stable`) and keeps each valid low in reset; the same
  instance asserts the network's responses (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The
  T6 and T8 items on this top read the port through its covers alone and
  take no handshake assumption. `asm_ctp_peer_four_phase` models the far end
  of external port 0 on
  the synchronized, de-inverted request and acknowledge (the far end
  acknowledges a request it sees and withdraws the acknowledge after the
  request drops, holds its own request until it sees the acknowledge, and
  raises no new one while the acknowledge is high); `asm_ctp_stretch_small`
  bounds port 0's `STRETCH_MULT` to 4. The pad inputs are otherwise free.

Every `cov_` label stays reachable under these assumptions; an unreached
cover fails the item, and an assumption change re-runs every cover of its
top (`formal-property-style.adoc`, cover reachability).

[[dtp-formal-depths]]
==== Tasks and depths

Each block has its own `bmc`, `cover` and `prove` task triple, named
`<block>_bmc`, `<block>_cover` and `<block>_prove`; the T8 item runs the
first two (<<dtp-formal-depths>>). On the `dtp` top the
triples share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, selected by task tags, and one app
table per block passes its triple in `args`, so `testlists/formal.toml` lists
one item per block. The `jtag2axi` and `cross_trigger_network` tops have their
own task files. The `prove` task carries no depth; `bmc` and `cover` share
one depth per block, set above the step at which the block's deepest cover
is reached. The table gives the step at which each block's deepest cover
is reachable and the depth set above it; the derivations that follow give
the structural latency of each route.

The bound follows the block's structural latency. Under `multiclock on` every
step is a time point and one `tck` cycle costs two steps; the reset
environment releases the resets at the first `tck` posedge, so a state that is
`n` `tck` edges from Test-Logic-Reset is first observable at step `3 + 2n`.
`clk_i` is a second explicit clock on the `dtp` top and toggles freely, so a
`clk_i`-domain latency of `k` cycles adds steps only where `2k` exceeds the
`tck` path. The `cross_trigger_network` top is single-clock and its depth is
in `clk_i` cycles. Two `tck` edge counts recur: an instruction load from
Test-Logic-Reset is 13 edges (Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Select-IR,
Capture-IR, Shift-IR, six shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-IR,
Update-IR), and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4` edges (Select-DR,
Capture-DR, Shift-DR, `b` shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-DR,
Update-DR).

[cols="1,4,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Deepest cover and the route the trace takes |Step |Depth
|T1 |`cov_tap_tlr_from_five_tms_high`: Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Capture-DR, then five TMS-high edges into Test-Logic-Reset |19 |30
|T2 |`cov_undefined_opcode_bypass`: the undefined opcode `0x0F` loaded, then a DR scan reaches Shift-DR through the bypass register; the thirty-one opcode covers each load one instruction |33 |40
|T3 |`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`: TMP_STATUS loaded and its escape bit written, CLAMP_HOLD loaded, BYPASS_ALT loaded with one shift, persistence off at the edge after Update-IR |67 |75
|T4 |`cov_stap_disabled_while_selected` on the extra STAP: TAP_3DCR loaded and the PTAP 3DCR select set in a scan of its own, one chain scan opens every SIB and a second sets every `stap_sel`, then the synchronized disable rises |67 |75
|T5 |`cov_sib_disabled_while_open` on the DFD SIB and the eight SIB patterns: SELECT_IJTAG loaded, a 3-bit DR scan writes the SIBs, the disable rises two edges later |41 |55
|T6 |`cov_dbgctl_boot_stall_ovrd_stall_high`: DEBUG_CONTROL loaded, a 5-bit DR scan sets both bits |37 |55 on `dtp`; the `ctn_clkstop_fpv` covers are reached at step 6 and its triple uses depth 10
|T7 |`cov_j2a_series_reads_admitted`: a CTRL update programs a read series and `FIFO_DEPTH + 1` data updates are admitted; a single write or read completes through the free response side at step 13 |23 |40; the `prove` task runs `abc pdr` on the model with the CDC in the loop, since k-induction cannot close `$past` relations under explicit clocks, where an induction window may hold a single `tck` edge
|T8 |`cov_hs_four_phase_complete`: a CSR write sets the mode, a source pulse starts the sender, the peer acknowledges and releases |18 |20; the item runs `bmc` and `cover` alone, since the block is P1 and signs off on a bounded result
|T9 |`cov_csr_ctp_read_after_write`: a write to CONFIG and a read of it complete through the crossbar and the register block |8 |12; the `prove` task also carries the shared AXI checker's assertions on the port
|===

A cover added at a greater distance raises its block's depth. The
derivations behind the table: an instruction load from Test-Logic-Reset is
13 `tck` edges and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4`, so the T2 and T3 routes
above cost 17 and about 33 edges; the T4 route costs 32 edges,
since the PTAP 3DCR select must be set before a chain scan reaches a SIB; T5 and T6
cost about 24 edges; the T7 machine completes a request in about five `tck`
cycles once its response side is free, while with the CDC in the loop the
clear handshake after reset lets nothing cross within sixty steps
(<<dtp-formal-assumptions>>); the CTN routes are single-clock cycle counts. The T1
depth also follows a diameter-plus-reset argument: depth 30 is thirteen
`tck` edges after reset release, the state-graph diameter of seven plus a
five-edge TMS reset sequence with one edge of margin.

[[dtp-formal-apps]]
==== App checks and path assignment

[cols="2,2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Check |Path |Targets |What it establishes
|Property checking (`fpv`) |Open, `sby` |T1 to T9 as listed |The properties above, with `prove` for the P0 set and bounded `bmc` plus `cover` at the block depth.
|Connectivity |Licensed connectivity app |T4, T5 and the static wiring of `dtp`: the STAP chain order I/O, SMC, SEP, extra, return; each `dbg_disable_i` field to exactly its path (the eleven fields of the simulation bench's 22-cell contract); the IC_RESET slice packing SMC, SEP, EXT to `reset_hold`; the pad-ring tie-offs on the unused CTP directions |Point-to-point wiring the property style cannot express as a single boolean without naming every net; on the open path the tie-offs and the chain order are constant assertions inside T4 and T5.
|CSR access policy from the register description |Licensed register app driven by the SystemRDL under `hw/ip/cross_trigger/*/regs/` |T9 |Every field's access, reset value and side effect for the sixteen CTPs and the CTM; the open path carries the address map and one slice.
|Security-path checks for the debug-disable gating |Open, `sby`, the P0 set of T4, T5 and T7 |T4, T5, T7 |With a disable field high, its path's select, TDO, host enables and bridge machine are quiet, and a release replays nothing; unbounded by `prove`.
|Reset X-safety |Licensed X-propagation app |T6, T7 |The two unreset paths named in <<dtp-formal-observations>>, and the JTAG2AXI CDC after an ACLK reset mid-transaction.
|Unreachability |Licensed unreachability app over the merged simulation coverage database, in the bench's environment |The code-coverage points the `all` regression leaves uncovered |The URG exclusion list `cov/config/vcs/dtp_unreachable.el` that the VCS coverage policy applies, and the check mode that fails when an RTL change makes one of its entries reachable or drops it (`DTP_FCOV.adoc`, Unreachability exclusion list).
|===

The licensed apps run through the same runner as site-supplied backends
(`run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification) and take the same property files as the
open path. The open tree carries no licensed collateral.

[[dtp-formal-running]]
==== Running and grading

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal                    # the smoke group: dtp_tap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items fpv        # every formal item
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items dtp_stap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --dry-run          # the rendered sby command
----

The `flist` stage regenerates `formal/build/dtp.f` from Bender, the filelist
all three task files read; the `formal` stage runs one item's `bmc`, `cover`
and `prove` tasks in one `sby` invocation whose work directories are
`<run>/stages/formal/<item>_<block>_<task>/`, for example
`dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc`. The `smoke` group holds `dtp_tap_fpv`; the `fpv` group
holds every item: `dtp_tap_fpv`, `dtp_ir_fpv`, `dtp_reset_fpv`,
`dtp_stap_fpv`, `dtp_sib_fpv` and `dtp_dbgctl_fpv` on the `dtp` top,
`jtag2axi_ctrl_fpv` on `jtag2axi`, and `ctn_clkstop_fpv`, `ctn_xtrig_fpv`
and `ctn_csr_fpv` on `cross_trigger_network`. The runner grades an item from
the task status files and JUnit reports, never from the exit code: every
task `PASS` with every cover reached is `PASS`; a counterexample, an
induction failure or an unreached cover is `FAIL` in the `formal_fail`
bucket, and `result.json` carries the `formal` object with the proven and
covered counts (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal result grading).

The FAIL path is checked by inverting one assertion body, for example
`dr_select_i == ir_select_i` in `ast_tap_select_exclusive`, and running the
same command: the `bmc` task reports `FAIL` with the counterexample trace at
`stages/formal/dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc/engine_0/trace.vcd`, the `prove` task
reports `FAIL`, and the item is `FAIL`. Every item's FAIL path is checked
the same way.

The open path needs a yosys-slang build with the concurrent-assertion
lowering and an SMT solver beside `sby` (`formal-property-style.adoc`,
Open-path toolchain); the runner guide's Formal verification chapter covers
the local and container runs and the licensed backends. Formal mode is a
developer-host flow: hosted CI runs no formal job.

[[dtp-formal-dependencies]]
==== Dependencies between targets

T3 and T4 hold the P0 set and stand alone on the `dtp` top. T7 and T9 take
the AXI and AXI-Lite handshake rules from the shared checker `ocah_axi_fv`,
bound with the environment's side assumed, and T1 takes the host's phase
rule from `ocah_jtag_fv`; the point-to-point peer model stays in the
`cross_trigger_network` environment, which T8 needs. The licensed
connectivity, register, X-propagation and unreachability apps take the same
property files; their configuration lives outside the open tree. A target
is complete when its item passes with every cover reached and its FAIL path
is checked.

=== Regression Groups

The simulation groups below come from `testlists/all.toml`;
`testlists/formal.toml` carries the formal item `dtp_tap_fpv` in its own
`smoke` group (<<dtp-formal-plan>>).

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Tests |Purpose
|`smoke` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test`, `dtp_jtag_tmp_status_register_smoke_test`, `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_single_write_read_test`, `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test` |Fast gate: one basic scenario per feature area
|`functional` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test`, and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI `single_write` and `single_write_read` scenarios |Fast functional regression
|`basic_jtag` |The Smoke and Basic JTAG scenarios |TAP, instruction, reset, BSR-loopback, and TMP smoke coverage
|`debug_tdr` |The TMP_STATUS, IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, and CAPS tests |Debug TDR coverage
|`all` |Every scenario-expanded test (`expected_count`-pinned in `testlists/all.toml`) |Full OCAH DTP regression
|`jtag2axi` |All JTAG2AXI write/read/error/robustness scenarios |AXI/AXI-Lite bridge coverage
|`scan` |STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scenarios |IEEE 1838 and IEEE 1687 coverage
|`dbg_disable` |The two debug-disable matrix tests plus every resource-specific directed gating test |Per-field `dbg_disable_t` closure (22-cell coverage contract)
|`xtrig` |XTRIG, CTP, and CTM scenarios |Cross-trigger and CSR coverage
|===

[[dtp-vplan-known-limitations]]
=== Known Limitations

Each row names the level that owns the behaviour the bench cannot reach
(<<dtp-vplan-scope>>); a PASS here closes the reachable clauses of an entry only.

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact
|iJTAG scan chains end in the bench's 4-, 5-, and 6-bit instrument stubs in `tb_top` |iJTAG scenarios validate the DTP-side SIB plumbing, chain length, and stub capture/update, not an external instrument network (integration level)
|The downstream STAP TAPs are behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` devices (5-bit IR, IDCODE, one writable TDR) attached only by the STAP-selection scenarios and the zero-length-bypass scenario |Other scan scenarios see the STAP host ports as wire loopbacks; the real SMC and SEP debug TAPs are integration level
|The extended STAP host scan interface ends in the bench's 7-bit host segment in `tb_top`, placed only by the extended-STAP scenario |Other scenarios see the host scan as a wire loopback; the external STAP chain is integration level
|The JTAG2AXI bridge ports end in bench responders that map every address |Fabric address decode and the default subordinate are SMC-level; the decode scenarios prove responder-injected DECERR handling on every bridge
|The DUT is elaborated from the bench configuration table, and the DFT ports are driven inactive |Entries are closed for that configuration; other DTP parameterizations are integration level, and DFT-mode behaviour is scan-insertion level
|The bring-up startup vector clears all debug disables |Gating behavior is only observable under explicit disable stimulus; the `dbg_disable` group drives directed and seeded disable vectors and closes the 22-cell coverage contract
|SV covergroups are not supported by Verilator |Under Verilator the `cov/sv/` modules record their cover-property points; their covergroups, the functional coverage model, are collected on VCS (see `DTP_FCOV.adoc`)
|===
